> AGENT

The error `4003: 'failed to connect to backend'` on port 22 usually means one of:

1. **The VM isn't running** — since the instance name suggests it was just created, it may still be booting or stopped
2. **SSH daemon isn't ready yet** — give it a minute after creation
3. **Missing firewall rule for IAP** — IAP needs an ingress rule allowing TCP:22 from `35.235.240.0/20`

Let's check the VM status and firewall rules:

```bash
# Check if VM is running
gcloud compute instances describe instance-20260313-103641 \
  --zone=europe-west1-c \
  --project=gen-lang-client-0249847738 \
  --format="get(status)"
```

```bash
# Check firewall rules for IAP
gcloud compute firewall-rules list \
  --project=gen-lang-client-0249847738 \
  --filter="direction=INGRESS" \
  --format="table(name, sourceRanges, allowed)"
```

If the VM is `RUNNING` but there's no firewall rule for `35.235.240.0/20`, create one:

```bash
gcloud compute firewall-rules create allow-ssh-iap \
  --project=gen-lang-client-0249847738 \
  --direction=INGRESS \
  --action=ALLOW \
  --rules=tcp:22 \
  --source-ranges=35.235.240.0/20
```

If the VM is `TERMINATED`/`STOPPED`, start it:

```bash
gcloud compute instances start instance-20260313-103641 \
  --zone=europe-west1-c \
  --project=gen-lang-client-0249847738
```

Try running those first two diagnostic commands and let me know what you see.

> AGENT

The issue is clear from the serial output. The VM has a **networking problem** — `dhclient` is repeatedly sending DHCPv6 Solicit messages but never getting a response. The VM can't get a proper network connection, which means SSH daemon likely never started or can't be reached.

Also notable: `OSConfigAgent` is hitting "context deadline exceeded", confirming network issues.

This is likely because the VM **doesn't have an external IP and the subnet isn't properly configured for internal-only networking**. Let me check:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01G2MVb326ehPVCZvmi9b8sw
```json
{
  "command": "gcloud compute instances describe instance-20260313-103641 --zone=europe-west1-c --project=gen-lang-client-0249847738 --format=\"get(networkInterfaces[0].accessConfigs,networkInterfaces[0].networkIP,networkInterfaces[0].subnetwork)\" 2>&1",
  "description": "Check VM network config"
}
```

> TOOL

tool_result
id: toolu_01G2MVb326ehPVCZvmi9b8sw
```
	10.132.0.2	https://www.googleapis.com/compute/v1/projects/gen-lang-client-0249847738/regions/europe-west1/subnetworks/default
```

> AGENT

The VM has an internal IP (`10.132.0.2`) but **no external IP** (no access config). That's fine for IAP tunneling, but the DHCPv6 failures suggest the network interface isn't fully initializing.

The quickest fix — **stop and restart the VM** to force a clean network initialization:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01FK7qTLq7tUTaJqotL3M5WW
```json
{
  "command": "gcloud compute instances reset instance-20260313-103641 --zone=europe-west1-c --project=gen-lang-client-0249847738 2>&1",
  "description": "Reset the VM to fix networking"
}
```

> TOOL


> AGENT

VM is resetting. Wait about 30-60 seconds, then try SSH again:

```bash
gcloud compute ssh --zone "europe-west1-c" "instance-20260313-103641" --tunnel-through-iap --project "gen-lang-client-0249847738"
```

If it still fails after the reset, the DHCPv6 issue might be persistent. In that case we could try adding an ephemeral external IP or checking if the subnet's DHCP is misconfigured.

> AGENT

Done. Private Google Access is now enabled on the `default` subnet in `europe-west1`. Your VM should be able to reach Google APIs and services (including package repos at `packages.cloud.google.com`) without an external IP.

Note: this only covers Google services. If you need to reach non-Google URLs (e.g., `nvidia.com` for drivers), you'll either need a **Cloud NAT** or an **external IP**. For GPU driver installs, Google hosts them at `packages.cloud.google.com` so Private Google Access should suffice. Let me know if curl still fails.

Can we add eggress to github?

> AGENT

There's no way to allowlist egress to specific domains like GitHub without an external IP — you need general internet egress. The cleanest option is **Cloud NAT**:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01Q9GgnrAfo5iuF1D2VZqTih
```json
{
  "command": "gcloud compute routers create nat-router --region=europe-west1 --network=default --project=gen-lang-client-0249847738 2>&1",
  "description": "Create Cloud Router for NAT"
}
```

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01Qaa1WGh3V7SdicqmEn8S8w
```json
{
  "command": "gcloud compute routers nats create nat-config --router=nat-router --region=europe-west1 --auto-allocate-nat-external-ips --nat-all-subnet-ip-ranges --project=gen-lang-client-0249847738 2>&1",
  "description": "Create Cloud NAT config"
}
```

```
     3→description: The hypernetwork carries the ENTIRE project lifecycle (methodology, plan, code progress, test results) through adapter weights. The prompt is just a launch trigger and NEVER changes. Each iteration's output gets encoded into an adapter for the next iteration.
     4→type: feedback
     5→---
     6→
     7→The hypernetwork is NOT just for retry context within a single task. It is the ENTIRE state machine for a project.
     8→
     9→Flow:
    10→- Iteration 0 (bootstrap): methodology doc → H() → methodology_adapter
    11→  (model knows HOW to plan/code/validate)
    12→- Iteration 1: project_prompt + methodology_adapter → model outputs implementation plan → H(plan) → plan_adapter
    13→- Iteration 2: same project_prompt + plan_adapter → model codes task 1 → execute → H(code+results) → progress_v1
    14→- Iteration 3: same project_prompt + progress_v1 → model codes task 2 (knows task 1 done) → H() → progress_v2
    15→- ...
    16→
    17→Key principles:
    18→1. The PROMPT never changes — it's just the project definition, a launch trigger
    19→2. ALL accumulated context (methodology, plan, progress, errors, what worked) lives in adapter weights
    20→3. The hypernetwork converts each iteration's output into the next adapter
    21→4. Context window stays constant because nothing gets stuffed into the prompt
    22→5. The swarm distributes plan tasks across agents, each with their own adapter chain
    23→
```

```
   230→async def test_full_iteration_loop(checkpoint_path: str) -> None:
   231→    """Test: Full rune_runner iteration loop with hypernetwork."""
   232→    step(4, "Full iteration loop: prompt → generate → execute → H() → adapter → repeat")
   233→
   234→    # Configure provider
   235→    os.environ["INFERENCE_PROVIDER"] = "transformers"
   236→    os.environ["TRANSFORMERS_MODEL_NAME"] = MODEL_NAME
   237→    os.environ["TRANSFORMERS_DEVICE"] = "mps"
   238→
   239→    # Clear provider cache so new env vars take effect
   240→    from inference.factory import _clear_cache
   241→
   242→    _clear_cache()
   243→
   244→    from rune_runner import run_project
   245→
   246→    result = await run_project(
   247→        project_prompt="Build a Python statistics library with mean, median, mode, stdev, percentile",
   248→        max_iterations=3,
   249→        checkpoint_path=checkpoint_path,
   250→        base_model_id=MODEL_NAME,
   251→        device="cpu",  # Hypernetwork on CPU (small)
   252→    )
   253→
   254→    print(f"\n  Session: {result['session_id']}")
   255→    print(f"  Total iterations: {result['total_iterations']}")
   256→    print(f"  Final tests passed: {result['final_tests_passed']}")
   257→    print(f"  Adapter dir: {result['adapter_dir']}")
   258→
   259→    # Show per-iteration details
   260→    adapters_seen: list[str | None] = []
   261→    for it in result["iterations"]:
   262→        adapter = it["adapter_id"]
   263→        adapters_seen.append(adapter)
   264→        code = it["generated_code"]
   265→        lines = code.split("\n") if code else []
   266→        print(f"\n  --- Iteration {it['iteration']} ---")
   267→        print(f"    Adapter: {adapter or 'none'}")
   268→        print(f"    Tests passed: {it['tests_passed']}")
   269→        print(f"    Exit code: {it['exit_code']}")
   270→        print(f"    Code lines: {len(lines)}")
   271→        if lines:
   272→            for line in lines[:10]:
   273→                print(f"      {line}")
   274→            if len(lines) > 10:
   275→                print(f"      ... ({len(lines) - 10} more lines)")
   276→
   277→    # Verify adapter progression
   278→    print(f"\n  Adapter progression: {adapters_seen}")
   279→
   280→    # Check adapter files on disk
   281→    adapter_dir = Path(result["adapter_dir"])
   282→    if adapter_dir.exists():
   283→        subdirs = sorted(adapter_dir.iterdir())
   284→        print(f"  Adapter subdirs on disk: {[d.name for d in subdirs]}")
   285→        for d in subdirs:
   286→            if d.is_dir():
   287→                contents = list(d.iterdir())
   288→                print(f"    {d.name}/: {[f.name for f in contents]}")
   289→
   290→
   291→def run_e2e() -> None:
   292→    """Run the full end-to-end test."""
   293→    print("=" * 60)
   294→    print("  RUNE E2E TEST: Hypernetwork as Project State Machine")
   295→    print("=" * 60)
   296→    print(f"\n  Model: {MODEL_NAME}")
   297→    print(f"  Architecture: {NUM_LAYERS} layers, hidden={HIDDEN_DIM}")
   298→    print(f"  LoRA: rank={RANK}, targets={TARGET_MODULES}")
   299→
   300→    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
   301→        checkpoint_path = os.path.join(tmpdir, "hypernetwork.pt")
   302→        adapter_dir = os.path.join(tmpdir, "test_adapter")
   303→
   304→        # Step 1: Create checkpoint
   305→        create_hypernetwork_checkpoint(checkpoint_path)
   306→
   307→        # Step 2: Test hypernetwork generates adapter
   308→        adapter_path = test_hypernetwork_generates_adapter(checkpoint_path, adapter_dir)
   309→
   310→        # Step 3: Test provider loads and uses adapter
   311→        test_provider_loads_adapter(adapter_path)
   312→
   313→        # Step 4: Full iteration loop
   314→        asyncio.run(test_full_iteration_loop(checkpoint_path))
   315→
   316→    print("\n" + "=" * 60)
   317→    print("  E2E TEST COMPLETE")
   318→    print("=" * 60)
   319→
   320→
   321→if __name__ == "__main__":
   322→    run_e2e()
   323→
```

> AGENT

No, it won't auto-download checkpoints. The script creates a **randomly initialized** hypernetwork checkpoint from scratch (line 62-108) and saves it to a temp directory. It does `torch.save` with random weights, not `from_pretrained`.

However, **the Qwen model will be downloaded** — at step 3, `TransformersProvider` loads `Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B` via HuggingFace (line 178: `_load_model_if_needed()`), which will auto-download it from the Hub if it's not already cached. That's ~1GB.

So in summary:
- **Hypernetwork checkpoint**: created randomly in-memory, no download
- **Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B**: will download from HuggingFace if not cached (~1GB)
- **PEFT adapter**: generated on-the-fly by the hypernetwork, no download

```
     1→"""DocToLoraHypernetwork: Perceiver-based instant LoRA adapter generation.
     2→
     3→Generates rank-8 LoRA adapter weights from token IDs in a single forward pass.
     4→Distinct from the QLoRA gradient-descent path (Phase 21) — this produces adapters
     5→in <1s by cross-attending over token embeddings with learned latents.
     6→
     7→IMPORTANT: All GPU imports (torch, safetensors) are deferred inside function/method
     8→bodies per INFRA-05 pattern — this module is importable in CPU-only CI.
     9→
    10→Usage:
    11→    from model_training.hypernetwork import (
    12→        DocToLoraHypernetwork,
    13→        save_hypernetwork_adapter,
    14→    )
    15→
    16→    model = DocToLoraHypernetwork(input_dim=DEFAULT_VOCAB_SIZE)
    17→    weights = model(token_ids)
    18→    save_hypernetwork_adapter(weights, "/tmp/adapter", "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B")
    19→"""
    20→
    21→from __future__ import annotations
    22→
    23→import json
    24→from pathlib import Path
    25→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Sequence
    26→
    27→# Default vocabulary size for the hypernetwork input embedding.
    28→# Used when the tokenizer vocabulary size is not explicitly provided.
    29→DEFAULT_VOCAB_SIZE: int = 32000
    30→
    31→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    32→    import torch
    33→
    34→
    35→def _build_hypernetwork_class() -> type:
    36→    """Build and return DocToLoraHypernetwork as a real nn.Module subclass.
    37→
    38→    This function is called the first time DocToLoraHypernetwork is instantiated
    39→    so that torch.nn.Module is only imported when needed (INFRA-05 pattern).
    40→    """
    41→    import torch  # noqa: PLC0415
    42→    import torch.nn as nn  # noqa: PLC0415
    43→
    44→    class _DocToLoraHypernetwork(nn.Module):
    45→        """Perceiver-based hypernetwork that generates LoRA adapter weights.
    46→
    47→        Given a sequence of token IDs (from a document/trajectory), produces a
    48→        PEFT-compatible state_dict with lora_A and lora_B matrices for every
    49→        target_module across all transformer layers — in a single forward pass.
    50→
    51→        Architecture:
    52→        - Lightweight learned token embedding (not the 7B base model's embedding)
    53→        - Learnable latent array (num_latents x latent_dim)
    54→        - Cross-attention: latents attend over token embeddings
    55→        - Self-attention stack: process latents
    56→        - Linear head: project to all LoRA weight parameters
    57→
    58→        Args:
    59→            input_dim: Vocabulary size for the hypernetwork's own token embedding.
    60→            num_latents: Number of learnable latent vectors. Default: 32.
    61→            latent_dim: Dimensionality of each latent vector. Default: 256.
    62→            depth: Number of self-attention layers over latents. Default: 4.
    63→            heads: Number of attention heads. Default: 8.
    64→            rank: LoRA rank for generated adapters. Default: 8.
    65→            target_modules: LoRA target module names. Default: ("q_proj", "v_proj").
    66→            num_layers: Number of transformer layers in target model. Default: 28.
    67→            hidden_dim: Hidden dim of target model. Default: 4096. Used as
    68→                fallback when module_dims is not provided.
    69→            module_dims: Per-module output dimensions for GQA models where
    70→                q_proj and v_proj have different sizes. Maps module name to
    71→                (in_features, out_features). If None, all modules use
    72→                (hidden_dim, hidden_dim).
    73→        """
    74→
    75→        def __init__(
    76→            self,
    77→            input_dim: int,
    78→            num_latents: int = 32,
    79→            latent_dim: int = 256,
    80→            depth: int = 4,
    81→            heads: int = 8,
    82→            rank: int = 8,
    83→            target_modules: Sequence[str] = ("q_proj", "v_proj"),
    84→            num_layers: int = 28,
    85→            hidden_dim: int = 4096,
    86→            module_dims: dict[str, tuple[int, int]] | None = None,
    87→        ) -> None:
    88→            super().__init__()
    89→
    90→            self.rank = rank
    91→            self.hidden_dim = hidden_dim
    92→            self.target_modules: list[str] = list(target_modules)
    93→            self.num_layers = num_layers
    94→            self.num_latents = num_latents
    95→            self.latent_dim = latent_dim
    96→
    97→            # Per-module dimensions: (in_features, out_features)
    98→            # For GQA: q_proj is (hidden, hidden) but v_proj is (hidden, kv_dim)
    99→            if module_dims is not None:
   100→                self.module_dims = module_dims
   101→            else:
   102→                _default_dims = (hidden_dim, hidden_dim)
   103→                self.module_dims = dict.fromkeys(target_modules, _default_dims)
   104→
   105→            # Hypernetwork's own lightweight embedding — does NOT load the 7B model
   106→            self.token_embedding = nn.Embedding(input_dim, latent_dim)
   107→
   108→            # Learnable latent array: shape (num_latents, latent_dim)
   109→            self.latents = nn.Parameter(torch.randn(num_latents, latent_dim))
   110→
   111→            # Cross-attention: latents (Q) attend over token embeddings (K, V)
   112→            self.cross_attend = nn.MultiheadAttention(
   113→                latent_dim, heads, batch_first=True
   114→            )
   115→
   116→            # Self-attention stack to process latents
   117→            encoder_layer = nn.TransformerEncoderLayer(
   118→                latent_dim, heads, batch_first=True
   119→            )
   120→            self.self_attend = nn.TransformerEncoder(encoder_layer, num_layers=depth)
   121→
   122→            # Project flattened latents to all LoRA weight parameters.
   123→            # Each (layer, module) needs lora_A and lora_B with per-module dims.
   124→            total_params = 0
   125→            for m in target_modules:
   126→                in_f, out_f = self.module_dims[m]
   127→                # lora_A: (rank, in_features), lora_B: (out_features, rank)
   128→                total_params += num_layers * (rank * in_f + out_f * rank)
   129→            self.weight_head = nn.Linear(latent_dim * num_latents, total_params)
   130→
   131→        def forward(self, token_ids: torch.Tensor) -> dict[str, torch.Tensor]:
   132→            """Generate PEFT-compatible LoRA adapter weights from token IDs.
   133→
   134→            Args:
   135→                token_ids: Integer token ID tensor of shape (batch, seq_len).
   136→
   137→            Returns:
   138→                Dict mapping PEFT state_dict keys to weight tensors.
   139→                Keys follow:
   140→                  base_model.model.model.layers.{i}.self_attn.{module}.lora_{A|B}.weight
   141→                Returns first batch element (one adapter per trajectory).
   142→            """
   143→            batch_size = token_ids.shape[0]
   144→
   145→            # Embed token IDs with hypernetwork's own lightweight embedding
   146→            x = self.token_embedding(token_ids)  # (batch, seq_len, latent_dim)
   147→
   148→            # Expand latents for batch dimension
   149→            latents = self.latents.unsqueeze(0).expand(
   150→                batch_size, -1, -1
   151→            )  # (batch, num_latents, latent_dim)
   152→
   153→            # Cross-attention: latents attend over token embeddings
   154→            latents, _ = self.cross_attend(
   155→                latents, x, x
   156→            )  # (batch, num_latents, latent_dim)
   157→
   158→            # Self-attention stack over latents
   159→            latents = self.self_attend(latents)  # (batch, num_latents, latent_dim)
   160→
   161→            # Flatten latents
   162→            latents = latents.reshape(
   163→                batch_size, -1
   164→            )  # (batch, num_latents * latent_dim)
   165→
   166→            # Project to all LoRA weight parameters
   167→            weights = self.weight_head(latents)  # (batch, total_weight_params)
   168→
   169→            # Reshape into PEFT state_dict for first batch element (index 0)
   170→            state_dict: dict[str, torch.Tensor] = {}
   171→            offset = 0
   172→
   173→            for i in range(self.num_layers):
   174→                for module in self.target_modules:
   175→                    in_f, out_f = self.module_dims[module]
   176→                    a_size = self.rank * in_f
   177→                    b_size = out_f * self.rank
   178→
   179→                    # lora_A: shape (rank, in_features)
   180→                    key_a = (
   181→                        f"base_model.model.model.layers.{i}"
   182→                        f".self_attn.{module}.lora_A.weight"
   183→                    )
   184→                    state_dict[key_a] = weights[0, offset : offset + a_size].reshape(
   185→                        self.rank, in_f
   186→                    )
   187→                    offset += a_size
   188→
   189→                    # lora_B: shape (out_features, rank)
   190→                    key_b = (
   191→                        f"base_model.model.model.layers.{i}"
   192→                        f".self_attn.{module}.lora_B.weight"
   193→                    )
   194→                    state_dict[key_b] = weights[0, offset : offset + b_size].reshape(
   195→                        out_f, self.rank
   196→                    )
   197→                    offset += b_size
   198→
   199→            return state_dict
   200→
   201→    return _DocToLoraHypernetwork
   202→
   203→
   204→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   205→# DocToLoraHypernetwork: lazy proxy that builds the real nn.Module class on
   206→# first instantiation. This keeps the module importable without torch.
   207→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   208→
   209→
   210→class _LazyHypernetworkProxy:
   211→    """Lazy proxy for DocToLoraHypernetwork.
   212→
   213→    Acts as a callable that creates a real nn.Module instance on first call.
   214→    The class is rebuilt (and cached) on first instantiation so that torch is
   215→    only imported when actually needed (INFRA-05 pattern).
   216→    """
   217→
   218→    _real_class: type | None = None
   219→
   220→    def __call__(self, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
   221→        if self._real_class is None:
   222→            self.__class__._real_class = _build_hypernetwork_class()
   223→        return self._real_class(*args, **kwargs)  # type: ignore[misc]
   224→
   225→    def __instancecheck__(self, instance: object) -> bool:
   226→        if self._real_class is None:
   227→            return False
   228→        return isinstance(instance, self._real_class)
   229→
   230→
   231→DocToLoraHypernetwork: _LazyHypernetworkProxy = _LazyHypernetworkProxy()
   232→
   233→
   234→def load_pretrained(
   235→    checkpoint_path: str,
   236→    device: str = "cpu",
   237→    **kwargs: Any,
   238→) -> Any:
   239→    """Load a pretrained DocToLoraHypernetwork from a checkpoint.
   240→
   241→    Args:
   242→        checkpoint_path: Path to the .pt checkpoint file.
   243→        device: Device to load onto ('cpu', 'cuda', 'mps'). Default: 'cpu'.
   244→        **kwargs: Override constructor args (input_dim, num_latents, etc.).
   245→
   246→    Returns:
   247→        DocToLoraHypernetwork nn.Module loaded with pretrained weights.
   248→    """
   249→    import torch  # noqa: PLC0415
   250→
   251→    checkpoint = torch.load(checkpoint_path, map_location=device, weights_only=True)
   252→
   253→    # Extract constructor args from checkpoint or use defaults/overrides
   254→    ctor_args = checkpoint.get("hypernetwork_config", {})
   255→    ctor_args.update(kwargs)
   256→    if "input_dim" not in ctor_args:
   257→        ctor_args["input_dim"] = DEFAULT_VOCAB_SIZE
   258→
   259→    model = DocToLoraHypernetwork(**ctor_args)
   260→    model.load_state_dict(checkpoint["model_state_dict"])
   261→    model = model.to(device)
   262→    model.eval()
   263→    return model
   264→
   265→
   266→def trajectory_to_tokens(
   267→    trajectory_text: str,
   268→    vocab_size: int = DEFAULT_VOCAB_SIZE,
   269→    max_length: int = 2048,
   270→) -> "torch.Tensor":
   271→    """Encode trajectory text as token IDs for the hypernetwork.
   272→
   273→    Uses a simple hash-based tokenization (character trigrams mapped to
   274→    vocab indices). This is intentionally simple — the hypernetwork learns
   275→    its own embedding, so exact tokenization doesn't matter as long as
   276→    it's consistent.
   277→
   278→    Args:
   279→        trajectory_text: Text to encode (plan, code diffs, test results, etc.).
   280→        vocab_size: Size of the hypernetwork's embedding vocabulary.
   281→        max_length: Maximum sequence length (truncates or pads).
   282→
   283→    Returns:
   284→        Token ID tensor of shape (1, max_length) ready for hypernetwork forward().
   285→    """
   286→    import torch  # noqa: PLC0415
   287→
   288→    tokens: list[int] = []
   289→    for i in range(0, len(trajectory_text) - 2):
   290→        trigram = trajectory_text[i : i + 3]
   291→        token_id = hash(trigram) % vocab_size
   292→        tokens.append(token_id)
   293→
   294→    # Pad or truncate to max_length
   295→    if len(tokens) < max_length:
   296→        tokens.extend([0] * (max_length - len(tokens)))
   297→    else:
   298→        tokens = tokens[:max_length]
   299→
   300→    return torch.tensor([tokens], dtype=torch.long)
   301→
   302→
   303→def generate_adapter(
   304→    hypernetwork: Any,
   305→    trajectory_text: str,
   306→    output_dir: str,
   307→    base_model_id: str,
   308→    vocab_size: int = DEFAULT_VOCAB_SIZE,
   309→    max_length: int = 2048,
   310→    device: str = "cpu",
   311→) -> str:
   312→    """End-to-end: encode trajectory, run hypernetwork, save adapter.
   313→
   314→    Args:
   315→        hypernetwork: A DocToLoraHypernetwork instance.
   316→        trajectory_text: Text to encode into the adapter.
   317→        output_dir: Directory to save the adapter files.
   318→        base_model_id: HuggingFace model ID of the base model.
   319→        vocab_size: Vocabulary size for tokenization.
   320→        max_length: Max token sequence length.
   321→        device: Device for tensor operations.
   322→
   323→    Returns:
   324→        Path to the saved adapter directory.
   325→    """
   326→    import torch  # noqa: PLC0415
   327→
   328→    # Infer vocab size from hypernetwork's embedding if available
   329→    h_vocab = getattr(
   330→        getattr(hypernetwork, "token_embedding", None), "num_embeddings", None
   331→    )
   332→    effective_vocab = h_vocab if h_vocab is not None else vocab_size
   333→    tokens = trajectory_to_tokens(trajectory_text, effective_vocab, max_length)
   334→    tokens = tokens.to(device)
   335→
   336→    with torch.no_grad():
   337→        weights = hypernetwork(tokens)
   338→
   339→    rank = getattr(hypernetwork, "rank", 8)
   340→    target_mods = getattr(hypernetwork, "target_modules", ["q_proj", "v_proj"])
   341→    save_hypernetwork_adapter(
   342→        weights,
   343→        output_dir,
   344→        base_model_id,
   345→        rank=rank,
   346→        target_modules=target_mods,
   347→    )
   348→    return output_dir
   349→
   350→
   351→def save_hypernetwork_adapter(
   352→    weights: dict[str, "torch.Tensor"],
   353→    output_dir: str,
   354→    base_model_id: str,
   355→    rank: int = 8,
   356→    target_modules: list[str] | None = None,
   357→) -> None:
   358→    """Serialize hypernetwork-generated LoRA weights in PEFT adapter format.
   359→
   360→    Writes:
   361→    - adapter_model.safetensors: the LoRA weight tensors
   362→    - adapter_config.json: PEFT-compatible configuration
   363→
   364→    Args:
   365→        weights: PEFT state_dict from DocToLoraHypernetwork.forward().
   366→        output_dir: Directory to write adapter files to (created if needed).
   367→        base_model_id: HuggingFace model ID of the base model.
   368→        rank: LoRA rank. Default: 8.
   369→        target_modules: List of module names. Default: ["q_proj", "v_proj"].
   370→
   371→    Note:
   372→        Does NOT include embed_tokens or lm_head — vLLM rejects these in adapters
   373→        (per Phase 21-01 decision: no modules_to_save in LoraConfig).
   374→    """
   375→    from safetensors.torch import save_file  # noqa: PLC0415
   376→
   377→    if target_modules is None:
   378→        target_modules = ["q_proj", "v_proj"]
   379→
   380→    output_path = Path(output_dir)
   381→    output_path.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   382→
   383→    # Write adapter weights as safetensors
   384→    safetensors_path = output_path / "adapter_model.safetensors"
   385→    save_file(weights, str(safetensors_path))
   386→
   387→    # Write PEFT-compatible adapter_config.json
   388→    adapter_config: dict[str, object] = {
   389→        "peft_type": "LORA",
   390→        "r": rank,
   391→        "lora_alpha": rank * 2,
   392→        "target_modules": target_modules,
   393→        "lora_dropout": 0.0,
   394→        "bias": "none",
   395→        "task_type": "CAUSAL_LM",
   396→        "base_model_name_or_path": base_model_id,
   397→        "inference_mode": True,
   398→        "modules_to_save": None,
   399→        "fan_in_fan_out": False,
   400→    }
   401→
   402→    config_path = output_path / "adapter_config.json"
   403→    config_path.write_text(json.dumps(adapter_config, indent=2))
   404→
```

```
     1→"""Bootstrap: add all workspace source directories to sys.path.
     2→
     3→Import this module at the top of any standalone script that needs to import
     4→from workspace packages without a full ``uv run`` invocation.
     5→
     6→Usage::
     7→
     8→    import sys
     9→    from pathlib import Path
    10→    sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
    11→    from bootstrap import setup_path; setup_path()
    12→"""
    13→
    14→from __future__ import annotations
    15→
    16→import sys
    17→from pathlib import Path
    18→
    19→_WORKSPACE_SRC_DIRS = [
    20→    "services/rune-agent/src",
    21→    "services/training-svc/src",
    22→    "services/evolution-svc/src",
    23→    "services/api-service/src",
    24→    "libs/inference/src",
    25→    "libs/shared/src",
    26→    "libs/model-training/src",
    27→    "libs/adapter-registry/src",
    28→    "libs/evaluation/src",
    29→]
    30→
    31→
    32→def setup_path() -> None:
    33→    """Insert all workspace src directories into ``sys.path`` (idempotent).
    34→
    35→    Determines the workspace root as the parent of the ``scripts/`` directory,
    36→    then prepends each ``src`` dir to ``sys.path`` if not already present.
    37→    """
    38→    root = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    39→    for rel_src in _WORKSPACE_SRC_DIRS:
    40→        p = str(root / rel_src)
    41→        if p not in sys.path:
    42→            sys.path.insert(0, p)
    43→
```

```
     1→#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2→# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     3→#  Rune — one-command dev environment setup
     4→#
     5→#  Usage:
     6→#    bash setup.sh                          # interactive
     7→#    RUNE_DIR=~/projects/rune bash setup.sh # custom location
     8→#    curl -fsSL <raw-url>/setup.sh | bash   # remote (non-interactive)
     9→# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    10→set -euo pipefail
    11→
    12→# ── Config ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
    13→REPO="ElixirTrials/rune"
    14→RUNE_DIR="${RUNE_DIR:-$HOME/rune}"
    15→REQUIRED_PYTHON="3.12"
    16→
    17→# ── Colors (respect NO_COLOR: https://no-color.org) ─────────
    18→if [[ -z "${NO_COLOR:-}" ]] && [[ -t 2 ]]; then
    19→    BOLD='\033[1m'    RESET='\033[0m'
    20→    RED='\033[0;31m'  GREEN='\033[0;32m'
    21→    YELLOW='\033[0;33m' CYAN='\033[0;36m'
    22→else
    23→    BOLD='' RESET='' RED='' GREEN='' YELLOW='' CYAN=''
    24→fi
    25→
    26→# ── Logging ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
    27→info()  { printf "${GREEN}[OK]${RESET}  %s\n" "$*" >&2; }
    28→warn()  { printf "${YELLOW}[!!]${RESET}  %s\n" "$*" >&2; }
    29→fail()  { printf "${RED}[FAIL]${RESET} %s\n" "$*" >&2; exit 1; }
    30→step()  { printf "\n${BOLD}${CYAN}▸ %s${RESET}\n" "$*" >&2; }
    31→
    32→# ── Banner ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
    33→printf >&2 "\n${BOLD}${CYAN}"
    34→cat >&2 <<'BANNER'
    35→  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    36→  │               R U N E                       │
    37→  │   AI agent + LoRA training + swarm infra    │
    38→  │   github.com/ElixirTrials/rune              │
    39→  └─────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    40→BANNER
    41→printf >&2 "${RESET}"
    42→printf >&2 "  This script will install system deps, authenticate with\n"
    43→printf >&2 "  GitHub, clone the repo, and set up the Python environment.\n\n"
    44→printf >&2 "  Install location: ${BOLD}%s${RESET}\n\n" "$RUNE_DIR"
    45→
    46→# Confirm if running interactively
    47→if [[ -t 0 ]]; then
    48→    read -rp "  Continue? [Y/n] " answer
    49→    case "${answer:-Y}" in
    50→        [Yy]*|"") ;;
    51→        *) echo "Aborted." >&2; exit 0 ;;
    52→    esac
    53→fi
    54→
    55→# ── OS Detection ────────────────────────────────────────────
    56→step "Detecting operating system"
    57→
    58→OS="$(uname -s)"
    59→DISTRO=""
    60→
    61→case "$OS" in
    62→    Darwin)
    63→        info "macOS detected"
    64→        ;;
    65→    Linux)
    66→        if [[ -f /etc/os-release ]]; then
    67→            # shellcheck source=/dev/null
    68→            . /etc/os-release
    69→            DISTRO="${ID:-unknown}"
    70→            info "Linux detected ($PRETTY_NAME)"
    71→        else
    72→            DISTRO="unknown"
    73→            warn "Linux detected but /etc/os-release missing — some installs may fail"
    74→        fi
    75→        ;;
    76→    *)
    77→        fail "Unsupported OS: $OS (this script supports macOS and Linux)"
    78→        ;;
    79→esac
    80→
    81→# ── Helper: check if a command exists ───────────────────────
    82→has() { command -v "$1" &>/dev/null; }
    83→
    84→# ── System Packages ─────────────────────────────────────────
    85→step "Installing system packages"
    86→
    87→if [[ "$OS" == "Darwin" ]]; then
    88→    if ! has brew; then
    89→        warn "Homebrew not found — installing..."
    90→        /bin/bash -c "$(curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Homebrew/install/HEAD/install.sh)"
    91→        # Add brew to PATH for Apple Silicon
    92→        if [[ -f /opt/homebrew/bin/brew ]]; then
    93→            eval "$(/opt/homebrew/bin/brew shellenv)"
    94→        fi
    95→        info "Homebrew installed"
    96→    else
    97→        info "Homebrew already installed"
    98→    fi
    99→
   100→    for pkg in git curl; do
   101→        if has "$pkg"; then
   102→            info "$pkg already installed"
   103→        else
   104→            brew install "$pkg"
   105→            info "$pkg installed"
   106→        fi
   107→    done
   108→
   109→elif [[ "$DISTRO" == "ubuntu" || "$DISTRO" == "debian" ]]; then
   110→    NEEDED=()
   111→    for pkg in git curl build-essential libssl-dev wget; do
   112→        if dpkg -s "$pkg" &>/dev/null; then
   113→            info "$pkg already installed"
   114→        else
   115→            NEEDED+=("$pkg")
   116→        fi
   117→    done
   118→    if [[ ${#NEEDED[@]} -gt 0 ]]; then
   119→        # Disable stale apt sources (e.g. archived bullseye-backports) that
   120→        # would make apt-get update fail with "no longer has a Release file".
   121→        fix_stale_apt_sources() {
   122→            local src_dir="/etc/apt/sources.list.d"
   123→            for f in /etc/apt/sources.list "$src_dir"/*.list; do
   124→                [[ -f "$f" ]] || continue
   125→                if grep -qE '(bullseye-backports|stretch|jessie)' "$f" 2>/dev/null; then
   126→                    warn "Disabling stale apt source: $f"
   127→                    sudo sed -i.bak '/bullseye-backports\|stretch\|jessie/s/^/#/' "$f"
   128→                fi
   129→            done
   130→        }
   131→        fix_stale_apt_sources
   132→
   133→        if ! sudo apt-get update -qq 2>/dev/null; then
   134→            warn "apt-get update had errors — retrying with --allow-releaseinfo-change..."
   135→            sudo apt-get update -qq --allow-releaseinfo-change || warn "apt-get update partially failed — continuing anyway"
   136→        fi
   137→        sudo apt-get install -y "${NEEDED[@]}"
   138→        info "Installed: ${NEEDED[*]}"
   139→    fi
   140→else
   141→    warn "Skipping system package install — please ensure git and curl are available"
   142→fi
   143→
   144→# ── GitHub CLI ──────────────────────────────────────────────
   145→step "Setting up GitHub CLI"
   146→
   147→if has gh; then
   148→    info "gh already installed ($(gh --version | head -1))"
   149→else
   150→    if [[ "$OS" == "Darwin" ]]; then
   151→        brew install gh
   152→        info "gh installed via Homebrew"
   153→
   154→    elif [[ "$DISTRO" == "ubuntu" || "$DISTRO" == "debian" ]]; then
   155→        sudo mkdir -p -m 755 /etc/apt/keyrings
   156→        curl -fsSL https://cli.github.com/packages/githubcli-archive-keyring.gpg \
   157→            | sudo tee /etc/apt/keyrings/githubcli-archive-keyring.gpg >/dev/null
   158→        sudo chmod go+r /etc/apt/keyrings/githubcli-archive-keyring.gpg
   159→        echo "deb [arch=$(dpkg --print-architecture) signed-by=/etc/apt/keyrings/githubcli-archive-keyring.gpg] https://cli.github.com/packages stable main" | \
   160→            sudo tee /etc/apt/sources.list.d/github-cli.list >/dev/null
   161→        sudo apt-get update -qq 2>/dev/null || true
   162→        sudo apt-get install -y gh
   163→        info "gh installed via apt"
   164→    else
   165→        fail "Cannot auto-install gh on this distro — install manually: https://cli.github.com"
   166→    fi
   167→fi
   168→
   169→# ── GitHub Authentication ───────────────────────────────────
   170→step "Authenticating with GitHub"
   171→
   172→if gh auth status &>/dev/null; then
   173→    info "Already authenticated with GitHub"
   174→else
   175→    printf >&2 "  You'll be prompted to log in. Choose ${BOLD}HTTPS${RESET} and follow the\n"
   176→    printf >&2 "  browser flow, or paste a personal access token.\n\n"
   177→    gh auth login
   178→    if gh auth status &>/dev/null; then
   179→        info "GitHub authentication successful"
   180→    else
   181→        fail "GitHub authentication failed — re-run this script to try again"
   182→    fi
   183→fi
   184→
   185→# ── Clone Repository ───────────────────────────────────────
   186→step "Cloning repository"
   187→
   188→if [[ -d "$RUNE_DIR/.git" ]]; then
   189→    info "Repo already exists at $RUNE_DIR — pulling latest..."
   190→    git -C "$RUNE_DIR" pull --ff-only || warn "Pull failed (you may have local changes) — continuing with existing code"
   191→else
   192→    gh repo clone "$REPO" "$RUNE_DIR"
   193→    info "Cloned to $RUNE_DIR"
   194→fi
   195→
   196→cd "$RUNE_DIR"
   197→
   198→# ── Install uv ─────────────────────────────────────────────
   199→step "Installing uv (Python package manager)"
   200→
   201→if has uv; then
   202→    info "uv already installed ($(uv --version))"
   203→else
   204→    curl -LsSf https://astral.sh/uv/install.sh | sh
   205→    # Make uv available in this session
   206→    export PATH="$HOME/.local/bin:$HOME/.cargo/bin:$PATH"
   207→    if has uv; then
   208→        info "uv installed ($(uv --version))"
   209→    else
   210→        fail "uv install succeeded but binary not found on PATH"
   211→    fi
   212→fi
   213→
   214→# ── Python + Dependencies ──────────────────────────────────
   215→step "Installing Python $REQUIRED_PYTHON and project dependencies"
   216→
   217→uv python install "$REQUIRED_PYTHON"
   218→info "Python $REQUIRED_PYTHON ready"
   219→
   220→# Phase 1: core deps (always succeeds — no GPU packages)
   221→uv sync
   222→info "Core workspace dependencies installed"
   223→
   224→# Phase 2: GPU extras (torch, peft, transformers, etc.) — best-effort
   225→step "Installing GPU dependencies (optional)"
   226→
   227→if uv sync --extra gpu 2>&1; then
   228→    info "GPU dependencies installed (torch, transformers, peft, etc.)"
   229→else
   230→    warn "GPU dependencies could not be installed"
   231→    warn "This is fine for CPU-only machines. To install later:"
   232→    warn "  cd $RUNE_DIR && uv sync --extra gpu"
   233→fi
   234→
   235→# ── Smoke Test ──────────────────────────────────────────────
   236→step "Running smoke tests"
   237→
   238→SMOKE_PASS=true
   239→
   240→# Use the venv python directly — more reliable than 'uv run' for import checks
   241→VENV_PYTHON="$RUNE_DIR/.venv/bin/python"
   242→if [[ ! -x "$VENV_PYTHON" ]]; then
   243→    warn "Virtual environment not found at $RUNE_DIR/.venv — trying 'uv run' instead"
   244→    VENV_PYTHON="uv run python"
   245→fi
   246→
   247→if $VENV_PYTHON -c "from shared.rune_models import TaskStatus; print('  shared lib:', TaskStatus.PENDING.value)" 2>&1; then
   248→    info "shared library OK"
   249→else
   250→    warn "shared library not importable — 'uv sync' may have failed"
   251→    warn "Try running: cd $RUNE_DIR && uv sync"
   252→    SMOKE_PASS=false
   253→fi
   254→
   255→if $VENV_PYTHON -c "from inference.provider import InferenceProvider; print('  inference provider ABC loaded')" 2>&1; then
   256→    info "inference library OK"
   257→else
   258→    warn "inference library not importable — 'uv sync' may have failed"
   259→    SMOKE_PASS=false
   260→fi
   261→
   262→if [[ "$SMOKE_PASS" == true ]]; then
   263→    info "All smoke tests passed"
   264→fi
   265→
   266→# ── Advisory Checks ────────────────────────────────────────
   267→step "Checking optional tools"
   268→
   269→if has docker; then
   270→    info "Docker found ($(docker --version | cut -d' ' -f3 | tr -d ','))"
   271→else
   272→    warn "Docker not found — install it to use the full stack (infra/docker-compose.yml)"
   273→fi
   274→
   275→if has nvidia-smi; then
   276→    GPU_INFO=$(nvidia-smi --query-gpu=name,memory.total --format=csv,noheader 2>/dev/null || true)
   277→    if [[ -n "$GPU_INFO" ]]; then
   278→        info "NVIDIA GPU detected: $GPU_INFO"
   279→    fi
   280→else
   281→    if [[ "$OS" == "Linux" ]]; then
   282→        warn "nvidia-smi not found — GPU training/inference requires NVIDIA drivers + CUDA"
   283→    fi
   284→fi
   285→
   286→# ── Done ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   287→printf >&2 "\n"
   288→printf >&2 "${BOLD}${GREEN}"
   289→cat >&2 <<DONE
   290→  ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   291→    Rune is ready!   ${RUNE_DIR}
   292→  ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   293→DONE
   294→printf >&2 "${RESET}"
   295→cat >&2 <<DONE
   296→  cd ${RUNE_DIR}
   297→
   298→  Run tests:       uv run pytest
   299→  Lint:            uv run ruff check .
   300→  Type check:      uv run mypy libs/ services/ scripts/ --ignore-missing-imports
   301→  Format:          uv run ruff format .
   302→  Full stack:      docker compose -f infra/docker-compose.yml up --build
   303→
   304→DONE
   305→
```

I see the two issues:

1. **safetensors import error** — it's in `[project.optional-dependencies] gpu`, so it's not installed unless you ran `uv sync --extra gpu`. On the VM you likely just ran `uv sync`.

2. **Auto-download hypernetwork** — I don't have a record of a specific HuggingFace checkpoint URL from a previous session, but I can add `huggingface_hub` download logic to the e2e script that downloads or creates the checkpoint automatically.

Let me fix both issues in the e2e script. The safetensors fix: use `torch.save` as fallback when safetensors isn't available. The auto-download: add a `download_or_create_checkpoint` function.

```json
  "new_string": "def download_or_create_checkpoint(checkpoint_path: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Download pretrained hypernetwork from HF, or create random if unavailable.\"\"\"\n    step(1, \"Obtaining hypernetwork checkpoint\")\n\n    cached = CACHE_DIR / HF_CHECKPOINT_FILENAME\n    if cached.exists():\n        print(f\"  Using cached checkpoint: {cached}\")\n        import shutil\n        shutil.copy2(cached, checkpoint_path)\n        print(f\"  Size: {os.path.getsize(checkpoint_path) / 1024:.1f} KB\")\n        return\n\n    # Try downloading from HuggingFace Hub\n    try:\n        from huggingface_hub import hf_hub_download  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n        print(f\"  Downloading from {HF_REPO}...\")\n        downloaded = hf_hub_download(\n            repo_id=HF_REPO,\n            filename=HF_CHECKPOINT_FILENAME,\n        )\n        CACHE_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        import shutil\n        shutil.copy2(downloaded, cached)\n        shutil.copy2(downloaded, checkpoint_path)\n        print(f\"  Downloaded and cached: {cached}\")\n        print(f\"  Size: {os.path.getsize(checkpoint_path) / 1024:.1f} KB\")\n        return\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"  HF download failed ({e}), creating random checkpoint instead\")\n\n    create_hypernetwork_checkpoint(checkpoint_path)\n\n\ndef create_hypernetwork_checkpoint(checkpoint_path: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Create a randomly initialized hypernetwork checkpoint.\"\"\"\n    import torch\n    from model_training.hypernetwork import DocToLoraHypernetwork\n\n    print(\"  Creating randomly initialized hypernetwork...\")"
```

```
Based on the search results, I found relevant information about Doc-to-LoRA:

Doc-to-LoRA (D2L) is a lightweight hypernetwork that meta-learns to perform approximate context distillation within a single forward pass. Given an unseen prompt, D2L generates a LoRA adapter for a target LLM, enabling subsequent queries to be answered without re-consuming the original context, reducing latency and KV-cache memory consumption during inference of the target LLM.

The research is available on Hugging Face as a paper resource. On a long-context needle-in-a-haystack task, D2L successfully learns to map contexts into adapters that store the needle information, achieving near-perfect zero-shot accuracy at sequence lengths exceeding the training context length.

For specific model checkpoints, the search results point to the academic paper page on Hugging Face (https://huggingface.co/papers/2602.15902), which may contain links to the actual model weights or code repository where you can find the checkpoint files.
```

```
Based on the search results, I found information about Doc-to-LoRA and hypernetworks on Hugging Face:

## Doc-to-LoRA Overview

Doc-to-LoRA (D2L) is a lightweight hypernetwork that meta-learns to perform approximate context distillation within a single forward pass. Given an unseen prompt, D2L generates a LoRA adapter for a target LLM, enabling subsequent queries to be answered without re-consuming the original context, reducing latency and KV-cache memory consumption during inference of the target LLM.

## Implementation Details

A Perceiver-based hypernetwork converts documents into rank-8 LoRA adapters for Ministral-3-3B-Instruct-2512 in sub-second time, enabling knowledge injection without context window overhead. Each LoRA is generated from a single document chunk (up to 2,048 tokens).

## Performance Results

On a long-context needle-in-a-haystack task, D2L successfully learns to map contexts into adapters that store the needle information, achieving near-perfect zero-shot accuracy at sequence lengths exceeding the target LLM's native context window by more than 4x.

The search results show that Doc-to-LoRA is a research project from Sakana AI available on Hugging Face, combining hypernetwork architectures with LoRA adapter generation for efficient context processing in language models.
```

```
The search successfully found the model page for **neopolita/doc-to-lora-ministral-3b-2512** on Hugging Face. Here's what the model is:

A Perceiver-based hypernetwork that converts documents into rank-8 LoRA adapters for Ministral-3-3B-Instruct-2512 in sub-second time, enabling knowledge injection without context window overhead.

It was ported from Sakana AI's Doc-to-LoRA (originally targeting Gemma-2-2B) to Ministral-3-3B. This hypernetwork powers Thoth, an agent that uses Doc-to-LoRA to alleviate context size pressure -- instead of feeding entire documents into the context window, it converts them into LoRA adapters on the fly, freeing up the context for reasoning and conversation while retaining document knowledge in the model's weights.

Doc-to-LoRA is a Perceiver-based hypernetwork (~309M parameters) that reads a document and generates a rank-8 LoRA adapter for Ministral-3-3B's MLP down_proj layers.

The model comes with some limitations: It was trained on a 10% subset of 4 compact QA datasets without the FineWeb QA dataset, and used 4,000 training steps versus ~20,000 in the original Gemma-2-2B training.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

```
| `qwen_4b_d2l/args.yaml` | Configuration |
| `qwen_4b_d2l/checkpoint-20000/pytorch_model.bin` | Model weights |

**Total Storage Used:** 6.5 GB

## Model Architecture & Base Models

This repository contains **LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) fine-tuned variants** of multiple base models:

1. **Gemma 2B** - Google's Gemma 2B model with LoRA adaptation
2. **Mistral 7B** - Mistral's 7B model with LoRA adaptation
3. **Qwen 4B** - Alibaba's Qwen 4B model with LoRA adaptation

The "d2l" likely refers to "documents-to-LoRA" adaptation, suggesting these are document-specific fine-tuned versions. Each variant includes:
- **args.yaml**: Training/inference arguments
- **checkpoint-xxxxx/pytorch_model.bin**: Fine-tuned model weights at specific training steps
```

```
     1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2→"""E2E test: SakanaAI doc-to-lora pretrained hypernetwork perceiver.
     3→
     4→Demonstrates the pretrained hypernetwork generates real LoRA weights from
     5→context by loading ONLY the perceiver/aggregator weights (not the full 5GB
     6→gemma-2-2b base models that ctx_to_lora normally requires).
     7→
     8→Steps:
     9→  1. Load checkpoint state_dict, extract perceiver weights
    10→  2. Build a standalone perceiver from the HypernetConfig
    11→  3. Pass synthetic context features -> generate LoRA weight dicts
    12→  4. Verify LoRA shapes match what PEFT expects for gemma-2-2b
    13→  5. Show that different contexts produce different LoRA weights
    14→
    15→Memory: ~1.5GB (vs ~12GB for the full ModulatedPretrainedModel)
    16→"""
    17→
    18→import sys
    19→import time
    20→from pathlib import Path
    21→
    22→import torch
    23→
    24→PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    25→CHECKPOINT_PATH = (
    26→    PROJECT_ROOT
    27→    / "trained_d2l"
    28→    / "gemma_demo"
    29→    / "checkpoint-80000"
    30→    / "pytorch_model.bin"
    31→)
    32→
    33→if not CHECKPOINT_PATH.exists():
    34→    print(f"Checkpoint not found: {CHECKPOINT_PATH}")
    35→    sys.exit(1)
    36→
    37→sys.path.insert(0, str(PROJECT_ROOT / "libs" / "shared" / "src"))
    38→from shared.hardware import get_best_device  # noqa: E402
    39→
    40→DEVICE = get_best_device()
    41→print(f"[e2e] Device: {DEVICE}")
    42→
    43→
    44→def load_checkpoint():
    45→    """Load checkpoint and extract hypernetwork config."""
    46→    print("\n[1/5] Loading checkpoint...")
    47→    t0 = time.time()
    48→    sd = torch.load(str(CHECKPOINT_PATH), map_location="cpu", weights_only=False)
    49→    elapsed = time.time() - t0
    50→    print(f"  Loaded in {elapsed:.1f}s ({len(sd)} keys)")
    51→
    52→    hc = sd["hypernet_config"]
    53→    print(f"  HypernetConfig: latent_size={hc.latent_size}, lora_r={hc.lora_config.r}")
    54→    print(f"  Base model: {sd['base_model_name_or_path']}")
    55→    print(f"  Target modules: {hc.lora_config.target_modules}")
    56→    ac = hc.aggregator_config
    57→    print(f"  Aggregator: {ac.num_blocks} blocks, {ac.n_latent_queries} latent queries")
    58→    print(f"  Layers: {len(hc.layer_indices)}, base_hidden_size: {hc.base_hidden_size}")
    59→
    60→    return sd
    61→
    62→
    63→def build_perceiver(sd):
    64→    """Build the HyperLoRA perceiver from checkpoint weights.
    65→
    66→    This extracts ONLY the perceiver/aggregator/head weights -- NOT the
    67→    two full gemma-2-2b copies that ModulatedPretrainedModel requires.
    68→    """
    69→    # Patch idefics2 for eager attention (no flash_attn on MPS)
    70→    import ctx_to_lora.modeling.idefics2 as idefics2_mod
    71→    from ctx_to_lora.modeling.hypernet import HyperLoRA
    72→    from ctx_to_lora.modeling.idefics2 import (
    73→        Idefics2Perceiver,
    74→        Idefics2PerceiverAttention,
    75→        Idefics2PerceiverConfig,
    76→        Idefics2PerceiverResampler,
    77→    )
    78→
    79→    idefics2_mod.IDEFICS2_PERCEIVER_ATTENTION_CLASSES["eager"] = (
    80→        Idefics2PerceiverAttention
    81→    )
    82→
    83→    # Patch eager attention to accept flash-only kwargs
    84→    _orig_fwd = Idefics2PerceiverAttention.forward
    85→
    86→    def _patched_fwd(self, *args, is_cross_attn=None, **kwargs):
    87→        kwargs.pop("cu_seq_lens_q", None)
    88→        kwargs.pop("cu_seq_lens_k", None)
    89→        kwargs.pop("max_length_q", None)
    90→        kwargs.pop("max_length_k", None)
    91→        return _orig_fwd(self, *args, **kwargs)
    92→
    93→    Idefics2PerceiverAttention.forward = _patched_fwd
    94→
    95→    # Patch resampler for eager mode (no flash_attn)
    96→    def _eager_resampler_forward(self, context, attention_mask=None, position_ids=None):
    97→        if position_ids is None:
    98→            bsz = context.shape[0]
    99→        else:
   100→            bsz = torch.where(position_ids == 0, 1, 0).sum().item()
   101→        latents = self.latents_q.unsqueeze(0).expand((bsz, *self.latents_q.size()))
   102→        compressed_context = latents
   103→        for layer in self.layers:
   104→            layer_outputs = layer(
   105→                latents=compressed_context,
   106→                context=context,
   107→                attention_mask=None,
   108→                position_ids=None,
   109→                past_key_value=None,
   110→                output_attentions=False,
   111→                use_cache=False,
   112→            )
   113→            compressed_context = layer_outputs[0]
   114→        return self.layernorm(compressed_context)
   115→
   116→    Idefics2PerceiverResampler.forward = _eager_resampler_forward
   117→
   118→    # Force eager on configs
   119→    _orig_perceiver_init = Idefics2Perceiver.__init__
   120→
   121→    def _patched_perceiver_init(self, enc_cfg, dec_cfg):
   122→        enc_cfg._attn_implementation = "eager"
   123→        enc_cfg._attn_implementation_internal = "eager"
   124→        dec_cfg._attn_implementation = "eager"
   125→        dec_cfg._attn_implementation_internal = "eager"
   126→        _orig_perceiver_init(self, enc_cfg, dec_cfg)
   127→
   128→    Idefics2Perceiver.__init__ = _patched_perceiver_init
   129→
   130→    _orig_cfg_init = Idefics2PerceiverConfig.__init__
   131→
   132→    def _patched_cfg_init(self, *args, **kwargs):
   133→        kwargs["attn_implementation"] = "eager"
   134→        _orig_cfg_init(self, *args, **kwargs)
   135→        self._attn_implementation = "eager"
   136→        self._attn_implementation_internal = "eager"
   137→
   138→    Idefics2PerceiverConfig.__init__ = _patched_cfg_init
   139→
   140→    print("\n[2/5] Building HyperLoRA perceiver (standalone, no gemma-2-2b)...")
   141→    t0 = time.time()
   142→
   143→    hc = sd["hypernet_config"]
   144→    hypernet = HyperLoRA(hc).to(torch.float32)
   145→
   146→    # Load ONLY the perceiver/aggregator/head weights from checkpoint
   147→    hypernet_keys = [
   148→        k for k in sd.keys() if k.startswith(("aggregator.", "head.", "extra_"))
   149→    ]
   150→    hypernet_sd = {}
   151→    for k in hypernet_keys:
   152→        if k in hypernet.state_dict():
   153→            hypernet_sd[k] = sd[k]
   154→
   155→    if not hypernet_sd:
   156→        # The checkpoint stores all keys including base_model/ctx_encoder
   157→        # HyperLoRA's state dict has aggregator.*, head.*, etc.
   158→        full_state = {k: v for k, v in sd.items() if isinstance(v, torch.Tensor)}
   159→        loaded = hypernet.load_state_dict(full_state, strict=False)
   160→        print(f"  Missing keys: {len(loaded.missing_keys)}")
   161→        print(f"  Unexpected keys: {len(loaded.unexpected_keys)}")
   162→    else:
   163→        loaded = hypernet.load_state_dict(hypernet_sd, strict=False)
   164→        print(f"  Loaded {len(hypernet_sd)} perceiver weight tensors")
   165→        if loaded.missing_keys:
   166→            print(f"  Missing keys: {len(loaded.missing_keys)}")
   167→
   168→    hypernet = hypernet.to(DEVICE)
   169→    hypernet.eval()
   170→
   171→    param_count = sum(p.numel() for p in hypernet.parameters())
   172→    elapsed = time.time() - t0
   173→    print(f"  HyperLoRA params: {param_count:,}")
   174→    print(f"  Built in {elapsed:.1f}s")
   175→    print(f"  Layer indices: {hypernet.layer_indices}")
   176→    print(f"  D_lora: {hypernet.d_lora}")
   177→
   178→    return hypernet, hc
   179→
   180→
   181→def generate_lora_weights(hypernet, hc):
   182→    """Pass synthetic context features through the perceiver to get LoRA weights."""
   183→    print("\n[3/5] Generating LoRA weights from synthetic context...")
   184→
   185→    # HyperLoRA.forward expects: (features, attn_mask, position_ids)
   186→    # With layer_to_layer=True (per_layer_activations ctx_encoder):
   187→    #   features shape: (batch, num_layers, seq_len, feature_dim)
   188→    # feature_dim = base_hidden_size (2304 for gemma-2-2b)
   189→    feature_dim = hc.base_hidden_size  # 2304
   190→    num_layers = len(hc.layer_indices)  # 26
   191→    seq_len = 64  # short for speed
   192→    batch = 1
   193→
   194→    # Simulate context features (as if from per-layer activations of gemma-2-2b
   195→    # running on a context document)
   196→    ctx_features = torch.randn(batch, num_layers, seq_len, feature_dim, device=DEVICE)
   197→    attn_mask = torch.ones(batch, seq_len, device=DEVICE, dtype=torch.long)
   198→
   199→    t0 = time.time()
   200→    with torch.no_grad():
   201→        lora_dict, layernorm_dict = hypernet.generate_weights(
   202→            ctx_features, attn_mask, None
   203→        )
   204→    elapsed = time.time() - t0
   205→    print(f"  Generated in {elapsed:.3f}s")
   206→
   207→    # Inspect generated LoRA weights
   208→    print(f"  LoRA dict modules: {list(lora_dict.keys())[:5]}...")
   209→    for mod_name, weights in list(lora_dict.items())[:2]:
   210→        print(f"    {mod_name}:")
   211→        for k, v in weights.items():
   212→            print(
   213→                f"      {k}: shape={v.shape}, dtype={v.dtype}, "
   214→                f"norm={v.float().norm():.4f}"
   215→            )
   216→
   217→    total_params = sum(v.numel() for w in lora_dict.values() for v in w.values())
   218→    print(f"  Total generated LoRA params: {total_params:,}")
   219→    print(f"  Modules: {len(lora_dict)}")
   220→
   221→    return lora_dict
   222→
   223→
   224→def verify_lora_shapes(lora_dict, hc):
   225→    """Verify generated LoRA shapes match expected format.
   226→
   227→    SakanaAI's HyperLoRA returns lora_dict with shape:
   228→      A: (batch, num_layers, rank, in_features)
   229→      B: (batch, num_layers, rank, out_features)
   230→    """
   231→    print("\n[4/5] Verifying LoRA weight shapes...")
   232→
   233→    lora_r = hc.lora_config.r
   234→    num_layers = len(hc.layer_indices)
   235→    target_modules = hc.lora_config.target_modules
   236→    feature_sizes = hc.feature_sizes  # ({down_proj: 9216}, {down_proj: 2304})
   237→
   238→    print(f"  Expected: rank={lora_r}, layers={num_layers}, targets={target_modules}")
   239→    print(f"  Feature sizes: in={feature_sizes[0]}, out={feature_sizes[1]}")
   240→
   241→    errors = []
   242→    for mod_name, weights in lora_dict.items():
   243→        if "A" in weights:
   244→            a_shape = weights["A"].shape
   245→            expected_in = feature_sizes[0].get(mod_name, 0)
   246→            # Shape: (batch, num_layers, rank, in_features)
   247→            if len(a_shape) == 4:
   248→                if a_shape[1] != num_layers:
   249→                    errors.append(
   250→                        f"{mod_name}.A: expected {num_layers} layers, got {a_shape[1]}"
   251→                    )
   252→                if a_shape[2] != lora_r:
   253→                    errors.append(
   254→                        f"{mod_name}.A: expected rank {lora_r}, got {a_shape[2]}"
   255→                    )
   256→                if a_shape[3] != expected_in:
   257→                    errors.append(
   258→                        f"{mod_name}.A: expected in_features {expected_in}, got {a_shape[3]}"
   259→                    )
   260→            print(f"  {mod_name}.A: {a_shape} (batch, layers, rank, in_features)")
   261→
   262→        if "B" in weights:
   263→            b_shape = weights["B"].shape
   264→            expected_out = feature_sizes[1].get(mod_name, 0)
   265→            if len(b_shape) == 4:
   266→                if b_shape[1] != num_layers:
   267→                    errors.append(
   268→                        f"{mod_name}.B: expected {num_layers} layers, got {b_shape[1]}"
   269→                    )
   270→                if b_shape[2] != lora_r:
   271→                    errors.append(
   272→                        f"{mod_name}.B: expected rank {lora_r}, got {b_shape[2]}"
   273→                    )
   274→                if b_shape[3] != expected_out:
   275→                    errors.append(
   276→                        f"{mod_name}.B: expected out_features {expected_out}, got {b_shape[3]}"
   277→                    )
   278→            print(f"  {mod_name}.B: {b_shape} (batch, layers, rank, out_features)")
   279→
   280→    if errors:
   281→        print(f"  ERRORS: {errors}")
   282→        return False
   283→
   284→    print(f"  All {len(lora_dict)} modules have correct shapes")
   285→
   286→    # Verify non-trivial A weights (B init to zero is expected for LoRA)
   287→    a_norm = sum(
   288→        weights["A"].float().norm().item()
   289→        for weights in lora_dict.values()
   290→        if "A" in weights
   291→    )
   292→    print(f"  A weight norm: {a_norm:.4f} (should be > 0)")
   293→    if a_norm < 1e-6:
   294→        print("  WARNING: A weights are near-zero")
   295→        return False
   296→
   297→    print("  Shape verification PASSED")
   298→    return True
   299→
   300→
   301→def compare_contexts(hypernet, hc):
   302→    """Show that different contexts produce different LoRA weights."""
   303→    print("\n[5/5] Comparing LoRA weights from different contexts...")
   304→
   305→    feature_dim = hc.base_hidden_size
   306→    num_layers = len(hc.layer_indices)
   307→    seq_len = 64
   308→    batch = 1
   309→
   310→    torch.manual_seed(42)
   311→    ctx_a = torch.randn(batch, num_layers, seq_len, feature_dim, device=DEVICE)
   312→    torch.manual_seed(123)
   313→    ctx_b = torch.randn(batch, num_layers, seq_len, feature_dim, device=DEVICE)
   314→
   315→    attn_mask = torch.ones(batch, seq_len, device=DEVICE, dtype=torch.long)
   316→
   317→    with torch.no_grad():
   318→        lora_a, _ = hypernet.generate_weights(ctx_a, attn_mask, None)
   319→        lora_b, _ = hypernet.generate_weights(ctx_b, attn_mask, None)
   320→
   321→    first_mod = list(lora_a.keys())[0]
   322→    a_weights = lora_a[first_mod]["A"]
   323→    b_weights = lora_b[first_mod]["A"]
   324→
   325→    diff = (a_weights - b_weights).float().norm().item()
   326→    a_norm = a_weights.float().norm().item()
   327→    b_norm = b_weights.float().norm().item()
   328→
   329→    print(f"  Module: {first_mod}")
   330→    print(f"  Context A weights norm: {a_norm:.4f}")
   331→    print(f"  Context B weights norm: {b_norm:.4f}")
   332→    print(f"  Weight difference norm: {diff:.4f}")
   333→    print(f"  Weights differ: {diff > 1e-6}")
   334→
   335→    if diff > 1e-6:
   336→        print("  PASSED: Different contexts produce different LoRA weights")
   337→        return True
   338→    else:
   339→        print("  FAILED: Contexts produced identical weights")
   340→        return False
   341→
   342→
   343→def main():
   344→    print("=" * 70)
   345→    print("  SakanaAI doc-to-lora E2E: Pretrained Perceiver LoRA Generation")
   346→    print("  (Standalone perceiver, no full gemma-2-2b needed)")
   347→    print("=" * 70)
   348→
   349→    total_t0 = time.time()
   350→
   351→    sd = load_checkpoint()
   352→    hypernet, hc = build_perceiver(sd)
   353→
   354→    # Free checkpoint to reclaim memory
   355→    del sd
   356→
   357→    lora_dict = generate_lora_weights(hypernet, hc)
   358→    shapes_ok = verify_lora_shapes(lora_dict, hc)
   359→    contexts_differ = compare_contexts(hypernet, hc)
   360→
   361→    total_elapsed = time.time() - total_t0
   362→    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
   363→    print("  RESULTS")
   364→    print("=" * 70)
   365→    print(f"  Shape verification: {'PASS' if shapes_ok else 'FAIL'}")
   366→    print(f"  Context differentiation: {'PASS' if contexts_differ else 'FAIL'}")
   367→    print(f"  Total time: {total_elapsed:.1f}s")
   368→    print("  Peak memory: ~1.5GB (vs ~12GB for full model)")
   369→    print("=" * 70)
   370→
   371→    return 0 if (shapes_ok and contexts_differ) else 1
   372→
   373→
   374→if __name__ == "__main__":
   375→    sys.exit(main())
   376→
```

```
    61→def create_hypernetwork_checkpoint(checkpoint_path: str) -> None:
    62→    """Create a randomly initialized hypernetwork checkpoint."""
    63→    import torch
    64→    from model_training.hypernetwork import DocToLoraHypernetwork
```

> AGENT

Here's the summary:

**`e2e_sakana.py`** — now auto-downloads the pretrained Sakana checkpoint from `SakanaAI/doc-to-lora` (`gemma_demo/checkpoint-80000/pytorch_model.bin`) via `huggingface_hub`, caches it locally at `trained_d2l/`. This is the real pretrained perceiver test.

**`e2e_test.py`** — cleaned up, uses random init (our own `DocToLoraHypernetwork` architecture targets Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B, which is incompatible with Sakana's gemma-2-2b checkpoint). This tests the full pipeline wiring.

**`pyproject.toml`** — `torch`, `safetensors`, `peft`, `transformers`, `huggingface-hub` moved to core deps. `uv sync` will install everything needed.

So on the VM: `uv sync` then `uv run scripts/e2e_sakana.py` for the pretrained Sakana test, or `uv run scripts/e2e_test.py` for the pipeline test.

```
Based on the search results, I found information about the SakanaAI `doc-to-lora` GitHub repository:

## Overview

Doc-to-lora is about hypernetworks that update LLMs to remember factual information. The project is located at the GitHub repository `SakanaAI/doc-to-lora`.

## Core Components

The project uses a module called `ctx_to_lora` (context-to-lora), which contains several key components:

- The `src/ctx_to_lora` directory includes modeling, data, tracking utilities, and configuration files
- The `ModulatedPretrainedModel` class is used for model loading and inference from checkpoints

## Technical Details

The project is named "ctx-to-lora" (version 0.0.1) with dependencies including transformers, deepspeed, accelerate, peft, and other machine learning libraries.

## Publication

This work is documented in a technical report titled "Doc-to-LoRA: Learning to Instantly Internalize Contexts" published by Sakana AI in February 2026.

The repository provides code for training hypernetworks that can dynamically generate LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) parameters to adapt language models to specific contexts and documents.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

> AGENT

Wrong approach. The right way is `constraint-dependencies` or just excluding ctx-to-lora's deps. Let me try the proper uv approach — `dependency-metadata` to override ctx-to-lora's declared deps:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_012eeQ2XP5fZJEzZ9kRYw6JX
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/pyproject.toml",
  "old_string": "[tool.uv]\noverride-dependencies = [\n    \"ctx-to-lora\",  # install without resolving its pinned deps (vllm, torch==2.6)\n]\n\n[tool.uv.workspace]",
  "new_string": "[[tool.uv.dependency-metadata]]\nname = \"ctx-to-lora\"\nversion = \"0.0.1\"\nrequires-dist = []  # ignore Sakana's pinned deps (vllm, torch==2.6, transformers==4.51)\n\n[tool.uv.workspace]"
}
```

```
    def __init__(self, config) -> None:
        super().__init__(config)
        self.num_blocks = config.num_blocks
        self.num_self_attn_per_block = config.num_self_attn_per_block
        self.shared_weights = config.shared_weights
        self.hidden_size = config.hidden_size
        self.hidden_act = config.hidden_act
        self.n_latents = config.n_latents
        self.rms_norm_eps = config.rms_norm_eps

        # Create Latents for Perceiver
        self.latents_q = nn.Parameter(torch.randn(self.n_latents, self.hidden_size))

        # First block
        assert config.num_blocks > 0
        first_x_attn = [Idefics2PerceiverLayer(config, is_cross_attn=True)]
        first_self_attn_block = [
            Idefics2PerceiverLayer(config, is_cross_attn=False)
            for _ in range(config.num_self_attn_per_block)
        ]

        self.layers = nn.ModuleList(first_x_attn + first_self_attn_block)
        for layer_idx in range(1, config.num_blocks):
            # cross-attention at the beginning of each block
            if self.shared_weights:
                if layer_idx == 1:
                    second_x_attn = Idefics2PerceiverLayer(config, is_cross_attn=True)
                x_attn = second_x_attn
            else:
                x_attn = Idefics2PerceiverLayer(config, is_cross_attn=True)
            self.layers.append(x_attn)

            # self-attention
            for i in range(config.num_self_attn_per_block):
                if self.shared_weights:
                    self_attn = first_self_attn_block[i]
                else:
                    self_attn = Idefics2PerceiverLayer(config, is_cross_attn=False)
                self.layers.append(self_attn)

        self.layernorm = Idefics2RMSNorm(self.hidden_size, eps=self.rms_norm_eps)

        self._use_flash_attention_2 = config._attn_implementation == "flash_attention_2"
        assert self._use_flash_attention_2
```

> AGENT

The assertion is at the very end of `__init__`. The config's `_attn_implementation` is set to something other than `"flash_attention_2"` by the time the resampler init runs. We need to patch the resampler's `__init__` to skip that assert:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_017QAen6rXrzXEdfxrwzPQAX
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/scripts/e2e_sakana.py",
  "old_string": "    Idefics2PerceiverResampler.forward = _eager_resampler_forward\n\n    # Force eager on configs\n    _orig_perceiver_init = Idefics2Perceiver.__init__",
  "new_string": "    Idefics2PerceiverResampler.forward = _eager_resampler_forward\n\n    # Patch resampler __init__ to skip flash_attention_2 assertion\n    _orig_resampler_init = Idefics2PerceiverResampler.__init__\n\n    def _patched_resampler_init(self, config):\n        config._attn_implementation = \"flash_attention_2\"\n        _orig_resampler_init(self, config)\n        # Override back to eager for actual forward pass\n        self._use_flash_attention_2 = False\n\n    Idefics2PerceiverResampler.__init__ = _patched_resampler_init\n\n    # Force eager on configs\n    _orig_perceiver_init = Idefics2Perceiver.__init__"
}
```

```
    def forward(
        self,
        latents: torch.Tensor,
        context: torch.Tensor,
        attention_mask: torch.Tensor | None = None,
        position_ids: torch.LongTensor | None = None,
        past_key_value: tuple[torch.Tensor] | None = None,
        output_attentions: bool = False,
        use_cache: bool = False,
    ) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor | None, tuple[torch.Tensor] | None]:
        """
        Runs Perceiver Self-Attention, with special (context, latents) appended along the `seq` dimension!

        Args:
            latents (`torch.Tensor`): Tensor of shape [bsz, n_latents, embed_dim] representing fixed length latents to compress to.
            context (`torch.Tensor`): Tensor of shape [bsz, seq, embed_dim] representing long-form context to resample.
            attention_mask (`torch.Tensor`, *optional*): Tensor of shape [bsz, 1, seq, n_latents] representing attention mask.
            position_ids (`torch.LongTensor`, *optional*): Tensor of shape [bsz, seq] representing position indices of each input token.
            past_key_value (`Tuple[torch.Tensor]`, *optional*): Tuple of tensors containing cached key and value states.
            output_attentions (`bool`, *optional*, defaults to `False`): Whether to return attention weights.
            use_cache (`bool`, *optional*, defaults to `False`): Whether to use past_key_value for caching.
        """
        bsz, q_len, _ = latents.size()
        kv_seq_len = q_len + context.size()[1]

        hidden_states = torch.concat([context, latents], dim=-2)

        query_states = self.q_proj(latents)
        key_states = self.k_proj(hidden_states)
        value_states = self.v_proj(hidden_states)

        query_states = query_states.view(
            bsz, q_len, self.num_heads, self.head_dim
        ).transpose(1, 2)
        key_states = key_states.view(
            bsz, kv_seq_len, self.num_key_value_heads, self.head_dim
        ).transpose(1, 2)
        value_states = value_states.view(
            bsz, kv_seq_len, self.num_key_value_heads, self.head_dim
        ).transpose(1, 2)

        past_key_value = getattr(self, "past_key_value", past_key_value)

        if past_key_value is not None:
            key_states, value_states = past_key_value.update(
                key_states, value_states, self.layer_idx
            )

        # repeat k/v heads if n_kv_heads < n_heads
        key_states = repeat_kv(key_states, self.num_key_value_groups)
        value_states = repeat_kv(value_states, self.num_key_value_groups)

        attn_weights = torch.matmul(
            query_states, key_states.transpose(2, 3)
        ) / math.sqrt(self.head_dim)

        if attn_weights.size() != (bsz, self.num_heads, q_len, kv_seq_len):
            raise ValueError(
                f"Attention weights should be of size {(bsz, self.num_heads, q_len, kv_seq_len)}, but is"
                f" {attn_weights.size()}"
            )

        if attention_mask is not None:
            if atten
```

The `Idefics2PerceiverLayer` is using `Idefics2PerceiverFlashAttention2` (the flash subclass) instead of the eager `Idefics2PerceiverAttention`. The attention class dispatch happens in `IDEFICS2_PERCEIVER_ATTENTION_CLASSES`. The existing patch sets `"eager"` but the config still resolves to `"flash_attention_2"`. Let me fix the patch to also map `"flash_attention_2"` to the eager class:

```
   234→def load_pretrained(
   235→    checkpoint_path: str,
   236→    device: str = "cpu",
   237→    **kwargs: Any,
   238→) -> Any:
   239→    """Load a pretrained DocToLoraHypernetwork from a checkpoint.
   240→
   241→    Args:
   242→        checkpoint_path: Path to the .pt checkpoint file.
   243→        device: Device to load onto ('cpu', 'cuda', 'mps'). Default: 'cpu'.
   244→        **kwargs: Override constructor args (input_dim, num_latents, etc.).
   245→
   246→    Returns:
   247→        DocToLoraHypernetwork nn.Module loaded with pretrained weights.
   248→    """
   249→    import torch  # noqa: PLC0415
   250→
   251→    checkpoint = torch.load(checkpoint_path, map_location=device, weights_only=True)
   252→
   253→    # Extract constructor args from checkpoint or use defaults/overrides
   254→    ctor_args = checkpoint.get("hypernetwork_config", {})
   255→    ctor_args.update(kwargs)
   256→    if "input_dim" not in ctor_args:
   257→        ctor_args["input_dim"] = DEFAULT_VOCAB_SIZE
   258→
   259→    model = DocToLoraHypernetwork(**ctor_args)
   260→    model.load_state_dict(checkpoint["model_state_dict"])
   261→    model = model.to(device)
   262→    model.eval()
   263→    return model
   264→
   265→
   266→def trajectory_to_tokens(
   267→    trajectory_text: str,
   268→    vocab_size: int = DEFAULT_VOCAB_SIZE,
   269→    max_length: int = 2048,
   270→) -> "torch.Tensor":
   271→    """Encode trajectory text as token IDs for the hypernetwork.
   272→
   273→    Uses a simple hash-based tokenization (character trigrams mapped to
   274→    vocab indices). This is intentionally simple — the hypernetwork learns
   275→    its own embedding, so exact tokenization doesn't matter as long as
   276→    it's consistent.
   277→
   278→    Args:
   279→        trajectory_text: Text to encode (plan, code diffs, test results, etc.).
   280→        vocab_size: Size of the hypernetwork's embedding vocabulary.
   281→        max_length: Maximum sequence length (truncates or pads).
   282→
   283→    Returns:
   284→        Token ID tensor of shape (1, max_length) ready for hypernetwork forward().
   285→    """
   286→    import torch  # noqa: PLC0415
   287→
   288→    tokens: list[int] = []
   289→    for i in range(0, len(trajectory_text) - 2):
   290→        trigram = trajectory_text[i : i + 3]
   291→        token_id = hash(trigram) % vocab_size
   292→        tokens.append(token_id)
   293→
   294→    # Pad or truncate to max_length
   295→    if len(tokens) < max_length:
   296→        tokens.extend([0] * (max_length - len(tokens)))
   297→    else:
   298→        tokens = tokens[:max_length]
   299→
   300→    return torch.tensor([tokens], dtype=torch.long)
   301→
```
