```
     1→"""Rune Runner: Outer iteration loop with hypernetwork state machine.
     2→
     3→The hypernetwork carries the ENTIRE project lifecycle state through weight-space.
     4→The prompt is the launch trigger and stays constant. Each iteration's output
     5→(plan, code, test results) gets encoded into an adapter via hypernetwork, and
     6→the NEXT iteration loads that adapter.
     7→
     8→Iteration 0 (bootstrap):
     9→  methodology doc → H() → methodology_adapter
    10→
    11→Iteration 1 (planning):
    12→  project prompt + methodology_adapter → plan output → H() → plan_adapter
    13→
    14→Iteration 2+ (coding/fixing):
    15→  project prompt + latest_adapter → code → execute → H() → next_adapter
    16→
    17→Usage:
    18→    uv run scripts/rune_runner.py --project "Build a Python statistics library"
    19→    uv run scripts/rune_runner.py --project "..." --max-iterations 10
    20→    uv run scripts/rune_runner.py --project "..." --model-path /path/to/model.gguf
    21→"""
    22→
    23→from __future__ import annotations
    24→
    25→import argparse
    26→import asyncio
    27→import logging
    28→import os
    29→import sys
    30→import uuid
    31→from pathlib import Path
    32→from typing import Any
    33→
    34→sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
    35→from bootstrap import setup_path
    36→
    37→setup_path()  # noqa: E402
    38→
    39→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    40→
    41→# Base model ID for adapter config
    42→DEFAULT_BASE_MODEL = "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-1.5B"
    43→METHODOLOGY_DOC_PATH = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "docs" / "rune-methodology.md"
    44→ADAPTER_BASE_DIR = Path.home() / ".rune" / "adapters"
    45→
    46→
    47→def _load_methodology() -> str:
    48→    """Load the methodology document for bootstrap iteration."""
    49→    if METHODOLOGY_DOC_PATH.exists():
    50→        return METHODOLOGY_DOC_PATH.read_text()
    51→    logger.warning(
    52→        "Methodology doc not found at %s, using inline fallback",
    53→        METHODOLOGY_DOC_PATH,
    54→    )
    55→    return (
    56→        "Phase 1: Decompose the project into tasks with dependencies.\n"
    57→        "Phase 2: For each task, generate code with a test suite.\n"
    58→        "Phase 3: Execute tests, validate, iterate on failures.\n"
    59→        "Phase 4: Integrate components, run the full test suite.\n"
    60→        "Output code as ```python blocks. Output plans as numbered lists.\n"
    61→    )
    62→
    63→
    64→def _build_trajectory_text(state: dict[str, Any], iteration: int) -> str:
    65→    """Build trajectory text from iteration state for hypernetwork encoding.
    66→
    67→    Args:
    68→        state: The RuneState dict after iteration execution.
    69→        iteration: Current iteration number.
    70→
    71→    Returns:
    72→        Trajectory text combining code, stdout, stderr, and test results.
    73→    """
    74→    parts = [f"=== Iteration {iteration} ==="]
    75→
    76→    if state.get("generated_code"):
    77→        parts.append(f"Code:\n{state['generated_code']}")
    78→    if state.get("stdout"):
    79→        parts.append(f"stdout:\n{state['stdout']}")
    80→    if state.get("stderr"):
    81→        parts.append(f"stderr:\n{state['stderr']}")
    82→    parts.append(f"tests_passed: {state.get('tests_passed', False)}")
    83→    parts.append(f"exit_code: {state.get('exit_code', -1)}")
    84→
    85→    return "\n\n".join(parts)
    86→
    87→
    88→async def run_iteration(
    89→    graph: Any,
    90→    project_prompt: str,
    91→    adapter_id: str | None,
    92→    session_id: str,
    93→    iteration: int,
    94→) -> dict[str, Any]:
    95→    """Run one iteration of the agent loop.
    96→
    97→    Args:
    98→        graph: Compiled single-iteration LangGraph.
    99→        project_prompt: The constant project definition prompt.
   100→        adapter_id: Current adapter to load, or None for base model.
   101→        session_id: Session ID for trajectory tracking.
   102→        iteration: Current iteration number.
   103→
   104→    Returns:
   105→        Final state dict from the graph execution.
   106→    """
   107→    initial_state = {
   108→        "task_description": project_prompt,
   109→        "task_type": "project",
   110→        "test_suite": "",
   111→        "adapter_ids": [adapter_id] if adapter_id else [],
   112→        "session_id": f"{session_id}-iter{iteration}",
   113→        "attempt_count": 0,
   114→        "max_attempts": 1,  # Single attempt per iteration
   115→        "generated_code": "",
   116→        "stdout": "",
   117→        "stderr": "",
   118→        "exit_code": 0,
   119→        "tests_passed": False,
   120→        "trajectory": [],
   121→        "outcome": None,
   122→    }
   123→
   124→    return await graph.ainvoke(initial_state)
   125→
   126→
   127→def run_hypernetwork(
   128→    trajectory_text: str,
   129→    output_dir: str,
   130→    base_model_id: str,
   131→    checkpoint_path: str | None = None,
   132→    device: str = "cpu",
   133→) -> str | None:
   134→    """Run the hypernetwork to produce a new adapter from trajectory.
   135→
   136→    If no checkpoint is available, logs a warning and returns None
   137→    (the system falls back to base model inference).
   138→
   139→    Args:
   140→        trajectory_text: Accumulated trajectory to encode.
   141→        output_dir: Where to save the adapter.
   142→        base_model_id: Base model identifier.
   143→        checkpoint_path: Path to pretrained hypernetwork checkpoint.
   144→        device: Device for computation ('cpu', 'mps', 'cuda').
   145→
   146→    Returns:
   147→        Path to saved adapter directory, or None if hypernetwork unavailable.
   148→    """
   149→    if not checkpoint_path or not Path(checkpoint_path).exists():
   150→        logger.warning(
   151→            "No hypernetwork checkpoint at %s — skipping adapter generation",
   152→            checkpoint_path,
   153→        )
   154→        return None
   155→
   156→    try:
   157→        from model_training.hypernetwork import (
   158→            generate_adapter,
   159→            load_pretrained,
   160→        )
   161→
   162→        hypernetwork = load_pretrained(checkpoint_path, device=device)
   163→        return generate_adapter(
   164→            hypernetwork=hypernetwork,
   165→            trajectory_text=trajectory_text,
   166→            output_dir=output_dir,
   167→            base_model_id=base_model_id,
   168→            device=device,
   169→        )
   170→    except Exception:
   171→        logger.exception("Hypernetwork adapter generation failed")
   172→        return None
   173→
   174→
   175→async def run_project(
   176→    project_prompt: str,
   177→    max_iterations: int = 10,
   178→    checkpoint_path: str | None = None,
   179→    base_model_id: str = DEFAULT_BASE_MODEL,
   180→    device: str = "cpu",
   181→) -> dict[str, Any]:
   182→    """Run the full project lifecycle as an iteration state machine.
   183→
   184→    Args:
   185→        project_prompt: Natural language project definition.
   186→        max_iterations: Maximum number of iterations.
   187→        checkpoint_path: Path to pretrained hypernetwork checkpoint.
   188→        base_model_id: HuggingFace model ID of the base model.
   189→        device: Device for hypernetwork computation.
   190→
   191→    Returns:
   192→        Summary dict with iteration results.
   193→    """
   194→    from rune_agent.graph import create_single_iteration_graph
   195→
   196→    graph = create_single_iteration_graph()
   197→    session_id = f"rune-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}"
   198→    adapter_dir = ADAPTER_BASE_DIR / session_id
   199→    adapter_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   200→
   201→    # Accumulated trajectory across all iterations
   202→    accumulated_trajectory = ""
   203→    current_adapter_id: str | None = None
   204→    current_adapter_path: str | None = None
   205→    iteration_results: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
   206→
   207→    # --- Iteration 0: Bootstrap (methodology → adapter) ---
   208→    logger.info("=== Iteration 0: Bootstrap ===")
   209→    methodology = _load_methodology()
   210→    accumulated_trajectory += f"=== Bootstrap ===\n{methodology}\n\n"
   211→
   212→    bootstrap_adapter_dir = str(adapter_dir / "iter0_methodology")
   213→    adapter_path = run_hypernetwork(
   214→        trajectory_text=accumulated_trajectory,
   215→        output_dir=bootstrap_adapter_dir,
   216→        base_model_id=base_model_id,
   217→        checkpoint_path=checkpoint_path,
   218→        device=device,
   219→    )
   220→    if adapter_path:
   221→        current_adapter_id = "methodology"
   222→        current_adapter_path = adapter_path
   223→        # Register adapter with the provider
   224→        from inference import get_provider
   225→
   226→        provider = get_provider()
   227→        await provider.load_adapter(current_adapter_id, current_adapter_path)
   228→        logger.info("Bootstrap adapter loaded: %s", current_adapter_id)
   229→    else:
   230→        logger.info("No hypernetwork checkpoint — running with base model only")
   231→
   232→    # --- Iterations 1..N: Planning then coding ---
   233→    for iteration in range(1, max_iterations + 1):
   234→        logger.info(
   235→            "=== Iteration %d/%d (adapter=%s) ===",
   236→            iteration,
   237→            max_iterations,
   238→            current_adapter_id or "none",
   239→        )
   240→
   241→        # Run one iteration with constant project prompt
   242→        state = await run_iteration(
   243→            graph=graph,
   244→            project_prompt=project_prompt,
   245→            adapter_id=current_adapter_id,
   246→            session_id=session_id,
   247→            iteration=iteration,
   248→        )
   249→
   250→        # Record result
   251→        result = {
   252→            "iteration": iteration,
   253→            "adapter_id": current_adapter_id,
   254→            "tests_passed": state.get("tests_passed", False),
   255→            "exit_code": state.get("exit_code", -1),
   256→            "generated_code": state.get("generated_code", ""),
   257→        }
   258→        iteration_results.append(result)
   259→
   260→        # Build trajectory from this iteration's output
   261→        iter_trajectory = _build_trajectory_text(state, iteration)
   262→        accumulated_trajectory += iter_trajectory + "\n\n"
   263→
   264→        # Check if project is complete (tests pass)
   265→        if state.get("tests_passed"):
   266→            logger.info("Project completed at iteration %d!", iteration)
   267→            break
   268→
   269→        # Run hypernetwork on accumulated trajectory → new adapter
   270→        iter_adapter_dir = str(adapter_dir / f"iter{iteration}")
   271→        new_adapter_path = run_hypernetwork(
   272→            trajectory_text=accumulated_trajectory,
   273→            output_dir=iter_adapter_dir,
   274→            base_model_id=base_model_id,
   275→            checkpoint_path=checkpoint_path,
   276→            device=device,
   277→        )
   278→
   279→        if new_adapter_path:
   280→            new_adapter_id = f"progress_v{iteration}"
   281→            from inference import get_provider
   282→
   283→            provider = get_provider()
   284→            # Unload previous adapter if any
   285→            if current_adapter_id:
   286→                try:
   287→                    await provider.unload_adapter(current_adapter_id)
   288→                except Exception:
   289→                    pass
   290→            # Load new adapter
   291→            await provider.load_adapter(new_adapter_id, new_adapter_path)
   292→            current_adapter_id = new_adapter_id
   293→            current_adapter_path = new_adapter_path
   294→            logger.info("New adapter loaded: %s", current_adapter_id)
   295→
   296→    return {
   297→        "session_id": session_id,
   298→        "total_iterations": len(iteration_results),
   299→        "project_prompt": project_prompt,
   300→        "final_tests_passed": iteration_results[-1]["tests_passed"]
   301→        if iteration_results
   302→        else False,
   303→        "iterations": iteration_results,
   304→        "adapter_dir": str(adapter_dir),
   305→    }
   306→
   307→
   308→def main() -> None:
   309→    """CLI entry point for the Rune iteration runner."""
   310→    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
   311→        description="Rune E2E Runner — Hypernetwork iteration state machine"
   312→    )
   313→    parser.add_argument(
   314→        "--project",
   315→        required=True,
   316→        help="Project definition (natural language prompt)",
   317→    )
   318→    parser.add_argument(
   319→        "--max-iterations",
   320→        type=int,
   321→        default=10,
   322→        help="Maximum number of iterations (default: 10)",
   323→    )
   324→    parser.add_argument(
   325→        "--checkpoint",
   326→        default=None,
   327→        help="Path to pretrained hypernetwork checkpoint (.pt)",
   328→    )
   329→    parser.add_argument(
   330→        "--model-path",
   331→        default=None,
   332→        help="Path to GGUF model (sets LLAMACPP_MODEL_PATH)",
   333→    )
   334→    parser.add_argument(
   335→        "--base-model-id",
   336→        default=DEFAULT_BASE_MODEL,
   337→        help="HuggingFace model ID for adapter config",
   338→    )
   339→    parser.add_argument(
   340→        "--device",
   341→        default="mps",
   342→        help="Device for hypernetwork computation (cpu, mps, cuda)",
   343→    )
   344→    args = parser.parse_args()
   345→
   346→    logging.basicConfig(
   347→        level=logging.INFO,
   348→        format="%(asctime)s %(name)s %(levelname)s %(message)s",
   349→    )
   350→
   351→    # Configure provider based on args
   352→    if args.model_path:
   353→        os.environ["INFERENCE_PROVIDER"] = "llamacpp"
   354→        os.environ["LLAMACPP_MODEL_PATH"] = args.model_path
   355→    elif "INFERENCE_PROVIDER" not in os.environ:
   356→        os.environ["INFERENCE_PROVIDER"] = "ollama"
   357→
   358→    result = asyncio.run(
   359→        run_project(
   360→            project_prompt=args.project,
   361→            max_iterations=args.max_iterations,
   362→            checkpoint_path=args.checkpoint,
   363→            base_model_id=args.base_model_id,
   364→            device=args.device,
   365→        )
   366→    )
   367→
   368→    logger.info("=== Run Complete ===")
   369→    logger.info("Session: %s", result["session_id"])
   370→    logger.info("Iterations: %d", result["total_iterations"])
   371→    logger.info("Final tests passed: %s", result["final_tests_passed"])
   372→    logger.info("Adapters saved to: %s", result["adapter_dir"])

   374→
   375→if __name__ == "__main__":

     1→"""TransformersProvider: InferenceProvider using HuggingFace transformers + PEFT.
     2→
     3→Loads models via AutoModelForCausalLM and applies LoRA adapters via PEFT.
     4→This is the only provider that natively supports PEFT-format adapters
     5→(safetensors) as output by the hypernetwork.
     6→
     7→IMPORTANT: transformers, torch, and peft are imported inside method bodies
     8→per INFRA-05 pattern so that this module is importable in CPU-only CI.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import logging
    14→from typing import Any

    16→from inference.provider import GenerationResult, InferenceProvider
    17→
    18→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    19→
    20→
    21→class TransformersProvider(InferenceProvider):
    22→    """InferenceProvider backed by HuggingFace transformers with PEFT LoRA.
    23→
    24→    Loads models locally via AutoModelForCausalLM. Adapters are applied
    25→    via PEFT's PeftModel, which natively reads the safetensors format
    26→    output by the hypernetwork.
    27→
    28→    Args:
    29→        model_name: HuggingFace model ID or local path.
    30→        device: Device to load model onto ('cpu', 'mps', 'cuda').
    31→        torch_dtype: Model dtype ('auto', 'float16', 'bfloat16').
    32→
    33→    Example:
    34→        >>> provider = TransformersProvider(model_name="Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B")
    35→        >>> result = await provider.generate("def hello", model="ignored")
    36→    """
    37→
    38→    def __init__(
    39→        self,
    40→        model_name: str = "",
    41→        device: str = "cpu",
    42→        torch_dtype: str = "auto",
    43→    ) -> None:
    44→        """Initialize TransformersProvider.
    45→
    46→        Args:
    47→            model_name: HuggingFace model ID or local path.
    48→            device: Device to load model onto.
    49→            torch_dtype: Model dtype string.
    50→        """
    51→        self._model_name = model_name
    52→        self._device = device
    53→        self._torch_dtype = torch_dtype
    54→        self._model: Any = None
    55→        self._tokenizer: Any = None
    56→        self._base_model: Any = None
    57→        self._loaded_adapters: dict[str, str] = {}  # id -> path
    58→        self._active_adapter: str | None = None
    59→
    60→    def _load_model_if_needed(self) -> None:
    61→        """Load the base model and tokenizer if not already loaded."""
    62→        if self._model is not None:
    63→            return
    64→
    65→        from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer  # noqa: PLC0415
    66→
    67→        logger.info("Loading model: %s (device=%s)", self._model_name, self._device)
    68→
    69→        self._tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(self._model_name)
    70→        if self._tokenizer.pad_token is None:
    71→            self._tokenizer.pad_token = self._tokenizer.eos_token
    72→
    73→        self._model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
    74→            self._model_name,
    75→            torch_dtype=self._torch_dtype,
    76→        )
    77→        self._model.to(self._device)
    78→        self._model.eval()
    79→        self._base_model = self._model
    80→        logger.info("Model loaded: %s", self._model_name)
    81→
    82→    async def generate(
    83→        self,
    84→        prompt: str,
    85→        model: str,
    86→        adapter_id: str | None = None,
    87→        max_tokens: int = 1024,
    88→    ) -> GenerationResult:
    89→        """Generate text using transformers with optional PEFT adapter.
    90→
    91→        Args:
    92→            prompt: The input prompt.
    93→            model: Ignored (model is set at construction).
    94→            adapter_id: LoRA adapter ID to activate. Must be loaded via
    95→                load_adapter() before use.
    96→            max_tokens: Maximum tokens to generate.
    97→
    98→        Returns:
    99→            GenerationResult with generated text and metadata.
   100→
   101→        Raises:
   102→            ValueError: If adapter_id is provided but has not been loaded.
   103→        """
   104→        import torch  # noqa: PLC0415
   105→
   106→        self._load_model_if_needed()
   107→
   108→        # Validate adapter before switching
   109→        if adapter_id and adapter_id not in self._loaded_adapters:
   110→            raise ValueError(
   111→                f"Adapter '{adapter_id}' has not been loaded. "
   112→                "Call load_adapter() first."
   113→            )
   114→
   115→        # Switch adapter if needed
   116→        if adapter_id and adapter_id != self._active_adapter:
   117→            self._activate_adapter(adapter_id)
   118→        elif not adapter_id and self._active_adapter:
   119→            self._deactivate_adapter()
   120→
   121→        inputs = self._tokenizer(
   122→            prompt, return_tensors="pt", truncation=True, max_length=2048
   123→        )
   124→        inputs = {k: v.to(self._device) for k, v in inputs.items()}
   125→        input_len = inputs["input_ids"].shape[1]
   126→
   127→        with torch.no_grad():
   128→            outputs = self._model.generate(
   129→                **inputs,
   130→                max_new_tokens=max_tokens,
   131→                do_sample=True,
   132→                temperature=0.7,
   133→                top_p=0.9,
   134→                pad_token_id=self._tokenizer.pad_token_id,
   135→            )
   136→
   137→        new_tokens = outputs[0][input_len:]
   138→        text = self._tokenizer.decode(new_tokens, skip_special_tokens=True)
   139→        total_tokens = outputs.shape[1]
   140→
   141→        return GenerationResult(
   142→            text=text,
   143→            model=self._model_name,
   144→            adapter_id=self._active_adapter,
   145→            token_count=total_tokens,
   146→            finish_reason="stop",
   147→        )
   148→
   149→    def _activate_adapter(self, adapter_id: str) -> None:
   150→        """Activate a loaded PEFT adapter.
   151→
   152→        Args:
   153→            adapter_id: The adapter name to activate. Must already be in
   154→                ``_loaded_adapters``.
   155→
   156→        Raises:
   157→            ValueError: If the adapter has not been loaded.
   158→        """
   159→        if adapter_id not in self._loaded_adapters:
   160→            raise ValueError(
   161→                f"Adapter '{adapter_id}' has not been loaded. "
   162→                "Call load_adapter() first."
   163→            )
   164→
   165→        from peft import PeftModel  # noqa: PLC0415
   166→
   167→        adapter_path = self._loaded_adapters[adapter_id]
   168→
   169→        if self._active_adapter:
   170→            # Already has a PEFT wrapper — load additional adapter
   171→            self._model.load_adapter(adapter_path, adapter_name=adapter_id)
   172→            self._model.set_adapter(adapter_id)
   173→        else:
   174→            # First adapter — wrap base model with PeftModel
   175→            self._model = PeftModel.from_pretrained(
   176→                self._base_model, adapter_path, adapter_name=adapter_id
   177→            )
   178→            self._model.to(self._device)
   179→            self._model.eval()
   180→
   181→        self._active_adapter = adapter_id
   182→        logger.info("Activated adapter: %s", adapter_id)
   183→
   184→    def _deactivate_adapter(self) -> None:
   185→        """Deactivate current adapter, revert to base model."""
   186→        if self._active_adapter and self._base_model is not None:
   187→            self._model = self._base_model
   188→            self._active_adapter = None
   189→            logger.info("Deactivated adapter, reverted to base model")
   190→
   191→    async def load_adapter(self, adapter_id: str, adapter_path: str) -> None:
   192→        """Register a PEFT adapter directory for use during generation.
   193→
   194→        The adapter directory must contain adapter_model.safetensors and
   195→        adapter_config.json as output by save_hypernetwork_adapter().
   196→
   197→        Args:
   198→            adapter_id: Unique name for the adapter.
   199→            adapter_path: Path to the PEFT adapter directory.
   200→        """
   201→        self._loaded_adapters[adapter_id] = adapter_path
   202→        logger.info("Registered adapter %s -> %s", adapter_id, adapter_path)
   203→
   204→    async def unload_adapter(self, adapter_id: str) -> None:
   205→        """Remove a registered adapter.
   206→
   207→        Args:
   208→            adapter_id: The adapter name to remove.
   209→        """
   210→        if adapter_id in self._loaded_adapters:
   211→            if self._active_adapter == adapter_id:
   212→                self._deactivate_adapter()
   213→            del self._loaded_adapters[adapter_id]
   214→            logger.info("Unloaded adapter %s", adapter_id)
   215→
   216→    async def list_adapters(self) -> list[str]:
   217→        """List all registered adapter IDs.
   218→
   219→        Returns:
   220→            Sorted list of registered adapter IDs.
   221→        """
   222→        return sorted(self._loaded_adapters.keys())

Good, now I have the full picture. Here's the plan:

The Sakana checkpoint needs the base model's per-layer activations as input. Our `load_pretrained` → `generate_adapter` pipeline needs to support this. The right approach:

1. Add a `SakanaHypernetwork` wrapper in our `model_training` lib that handles loading their checkpoint, patching flash attention, and providing the same interface as our `generate_adapter`
2. Update `load_pretrained` to detect Sakana checkpoints and return the right wrapper
3. Update `e2e_test.py` to use `google/gemma-2-2b-it` (matching the checkpoint) and auto-download

{
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/sakana_d2l.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"SakanaAI Doc-to-LoRA integration.\n\nWraps Sakana's pretrained HyperLoRA perceiver so it can be used through\nour hypernetwork interface (load_pretrained → generate_adapter).\n\nThe Sakana hypernetwork takes per-layer activations from a base model as\ninput and produces LoRA adapter weights.  This module handles:\n  - Downloading the checkpoint from HuggingFace\n  - Patching flash-attention assertions for CPU/MPS/non-flash environments\n  - Extracting per-layer activations from the base model\n  - Saving the generated LoRA weights in PEFT format\n\nGPU imports are deferred inside function bodies per INFRA-05 pattern.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport logging\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n# HuggingFace repo for Sakana's pretrained checkpoints\nHF_REPO_ID = \"SakanaAI/doc-to-lora\"\n# Available checkpoints: gemma_2b_d2l, gemma_demo, mistral_7b_d2l, qwen_4b_d2l\nDEFAULT_VARIANT = \"gemma_demo\"\nDEFAULT_HF_FILENAME = f\"{DEFAULT_VARIANT}/checkpoint-80000/pytorch_model.bin\"\nLOCAL_CACHE_DIR = Path.home() / \".cache\" / \"rune\" / \"sakana_d2l\"\n\n\ndef _patch_flash_attention() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Patch Sakana's idefics2 module to work without flash_attn.\n\n    Replaces flash attention classes and assertions with eager equivalents\n    so the perceiver can run on CPU/MPS/CUDA without flash_attn installed.\n    \"\"\"\n    import torch  # noqa: PLC0415\n    import torch.nn as nn  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n    import ctx_to_lora.modeling.idefics2 as idefics2_mod  # noqa: PLC0415\n    from ctx_to_lora.modeling.idefics2 import (  # noqa: PLC0415\n        Idefics2Perceiver,\n        Idefics2PerceiverAttention,\n        Idefics2PerceiverConfig,\n        Idefics2PerceiverResampler,\n    )\n\n    # Map both eager and flash_attention_2 to the eager attention class\n    idefics2_mod.IDEFICS2_PERCEIVER_ATTENTION_CLASSES[\"eager\"] = (\n        Idefics2PerceiverAttention\n    )\n    idefics2_mod.IDEFICS2_PERCEIVER_ATTENTION_CLASSES[\"flash_attention_2\"] = (\n        Idefics2PerceiverAttention\n    )\n\n    # Patch eager attention forward to accept flash-only kwargs\n    _orig_attn_fwd = Idefics2PerceiverAttention.forward\n\n    def _patched_attn_fwd(\n        self: Any, *args: Any, is_cross_attn: Any = None, **kwargs: Any\n    ) -> Any:\n        kwargs.pop(\"cu_seq_lens_q\", None)\n        kwargs.pop(\"cu_seq_lens_k\", None)\n        kwargs.pop(\"max_length_q\", None)\n        kwargs.pop(\"max_length_k\", None)\n        return _orig_attn_fwd(self, *args, **kwargs)\n\n    Idefics2PerceiverAttention.forward = _patched_attn_fwd\n\n    # Patch resampler forward to use eager path\n    def _eager_resampler_forward(\n        self: Any,\n        context: torch.Tensor,\n        attention_mask: torch.Tensor | None = None,\n        position_ids: torch.Tensor | None = None,\n    ) -> torch.Tensor:\n        if position_ids is None:\n            bsz = context.shape[0]\n        else:\n            bsz = torch.where(position_ids == 0, 1, 0).sum().item()\n        latents = self.latents_q.unsqueeze(0).expand((bsz, *self.latents_q.size()))\n        compressed_context = latents\n        for layer in self.layers:\n            layer_outputs = layer(\n                latents=compressed_context,\n                context=context,\n                attention_mask=None,\n                position_ids=None,\n                past_key_value=None,\n                output_attentions=False,\n                use_cache=False,\n            )\n            compressed_context = layer_outputs[0]\n        return self.layernorm(compressed_context)\n\n    Idefics2PerceiverResampler.forward = _eager_resampler_forward\n\n    # Patch resampler __init__ to bypass flash_attention_2 assertion\n    _orig_resampler_init = Idefics2PerceiverResampler.__init__\n\n    def _patched_resampler_init(self: Any, config: Any) -> None:\n        config._attn_implementation = \"flash_attention_2\"\n        _orig_resampler_init(self, config)\n        self._use_flash_attention_2 = False\n\n    Idefics2PerceiverResampler.__init__ = _patched_resampler_init\n\n    # Patch Perceiver init to force eager on sub-configs\n    _orig_perceiver_init = Idefics2Perceiver.__init__\n\n    def _patched_perceiver_init(self: Any, enc_cfg: Any, dec_cfg: Any) -> None:\n        enc_cfg._attn_implementation = \"eager\"\n        enc_cfg._attn_implementation_internal = \"eager\"\n        dec_cfg._attn_implementation = \"eager\"\n        dec_cfg._attn_implementation_internal = \"eager\"\n        _orig_perceiver_init(self, enc_cfg, dec_cfg)\n\n    Idefics2Perceiver.__init__ = _patched_perceiver_init\n\n    # Patch PerceiverConfig init to default to eager\n    _orig_cfg_init = Idefics2PerceiverConfig.__init__\n\n    def _patched_cfg_init(self: Any, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> None:\n        kwargs[\"attn_implementation\"] = \"eager\"\n        _orig_cfg_init(self, *args, **kwargs)\n        self._attn_implementation = \"eager\"\n        self._attn_implementation_internal = \"eager\"\n\n    Idefics2PerceiverConfig.__init__ = _patched_cfg_init\n\n\ndef download_checkpoint(\n    variant: str = DEFAULT_VARIANT,\n) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Download Sakana's pretrained checkpoint from HuggingFace.\n\n    Args:\n        variant: Which checkpoint variant to download.\n            Options: 'gemma_demo', 'gemma_2b_d2l', 'mistral_7b_d2l', 'qwen_4b_d2l'.\n\n    Returns:\n        Path to the downloaded checkpoint file.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Determine filename based on variant\n    if variant == \"gemma_demo\":\n        hf_filename = \"gemma_demo/checkpoint-80000/pytorch_model.bin\"\n    elif variant in (\"gemma_2b_d2l\", \"mistral_7b_d2l\", \"qwen_4b_d2l\"):\n        hf_filename = f\"{variant}/checkpoint-20000/pytorch_model.bin\"\n    else:\n        msg = f\"Unknown variant: {variant}\"\n        raise ValueError(msg)\n\n    cached = LOCAL_CACHE_DIR / variant / \"pytorch_model.bin\"\n    if cached.exists():\n        logger.info(\"Using cached Sakana checkpoint: %s\", cached)\n        return cached\n\n    from huggingface_hub import hf_hub_download  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n    logger.info(\"Downloading Sakana checkpoint %s from %s...\", variant, HF_REPO_ID)\n    downloaded = Path(hf_hub_download(repo_id=HF_REPO_ID, filename=hf_filename))\n\n    cached.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    import shutil  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n    shutil.copy2(downloaded, cached)\n    logger.info(\"Cached to: %s\", cached)\n    return cached\n\n\ndef load_sakana_checkpoint(\n    checkpoint_path: str | Path | None = None,\n    variant: str = DEFAULT_VARIANT,\n    device: str = \"cpu\",\n) -> tuple[Any, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Load Sakana's HyperLoRA perceiver from checkpoint.\n\n    Downloads from HuggingFace if no local path is provided.\n    Patches flash attention for CPU/MPS compatibility.\n\n    Args:\n        checkpoint_path: Path to local checkpoint. If None, downloads from HF.\n        variant: HF checkpoint variant (only used if checkpoint_path is None).\n        device: Device to load onto.\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (hypernet, hypernet_config).\n    \"\"\"\n    import torch  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n    _patch_flash_attention()\n\n    if checkpoint_path is None:\n        checkpoint_path = download_checkpoint(variant)\n\n    logger.info(\"Loading Sakana checkpoint: %s\", checkpoint_path)\n    sd = torch.load(str(checkpoint_path), map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=False)\n\n    hc = sd[\"hypernet_config\"]\n    logger.info(\n        \"HypernetConfig: latent_size=%d, lora_r=%d, base_model=%s\",\n        hc.latent_size,\n        hc.lora_config.r,\n        sd[\"base_model_name_or_path\"],\n    )\n\n    from ctx_to_lora.modeling.hypernet import HyperLoRA  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n    hypernet = HyperLoRA(hc).to(torch.float32)\n\n    # Load perceiver/aggregator/head weights from checkpoint\n    hypernet_keys = [\n        k for k in sd.keys() if k.startswith((\"aggregator.\", \"head.\", \"extra_\"))\n    ]\n    hypernet_sd = {k: sd[k] for k in hypernet_keys if k in hypernet.state_dict()}\n\n    if hypernet_sd:\n        loaded = hypernet.load_state_dict(hypernet_sd, strict=False)\n        logger.info(\"Loaded %d perceiver weight tensors\", len(hypernet_sd))\n        if loaded.missing_keys:\n            logger.info(\"Missing keys: %d\", len(loaded.missing_keys))\n    else:\n        full_state = {k: v for k, v in sd.items() if isinstance(v, torch.Tensor)}\n        loaded = hypernet.load_state_dict(full_state, strict=False)\n        logger.info(\n            \"Loaded full state: missing=%d, unexpected=%d\",\n            len(loaded.missing_keys),\n            len(loaded.unexpected_keys),\n        )\n\n    hypernet = hypernet.to(device)\n    hypernet.eval()\n\n    param_count = sum(p.numel() for p in hypernet.parameters())\n    logger.info(\"HyperLoRA params: %d\", param_count)\n\n    return hypernet, hc\n\n\ndef extract_activations(\n    text: str,\n    base_model_name: str,\n    layer_indices: list[int],\n    device: str = \"cpu\",\n    max_length: int = 512,\n) -> tuple[Any, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Extract per-layer hidden state activations from the base model.\n\n    Runs the input text through the base model and captures hidden states\n    at the specified layer indices. These activations are what Sakana's\n    HyperLoRA perceiver expects as input.\n\n    Args:\n        text: Input text to process.\n        base_model_name: HuggingFace model ID for the base model.\n        layer_indices: Which layers to extract activations from.\n        device: Device for computation.\n        max_length: Max token sequence length.\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (features, attention_mask) ready for HyperLoRA.\n        features shape: (1, num_layers, seq_len, hidden_dim)\n        attention_mask shape: (1, seq_len)\n    \"\"\"\n    import torch  # noqa: PLC0415\n    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n    logger.info(\"Loading base model %s for activation extraction...\", base_model_name)\n    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(base_model_name)\n    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(\n        base_model_name,\n        output_hidden_states=True,\n        torch_dtype=torch.float32,\n    )\n    model.to(device)\n    model.eval()\n\n    inputs = tokenizer(\n        text, return_tensors=\"pt\", truncation=True, max_length=max_length\n    )\n    inputs = {k: v.to(device) for k, v in inputs.items()}\n\n    with torch.no_grad():\n        outputs = model(**inputs)\n\n    # outputs.hidden_states is a tuple of (num_layers+1,) tensors\n    # each of shape (batch, seq_len, hidden_dim)\n    hidden_states = outputs.hidden_states\n\n    # Stack selected layers: (batch, num_layers, seq_len, hidden_dim)\n    selected = torch.stack([hidden_states[i] for i in layer_indices], dim=1)\n    attention_mask = inputs[\"attention_mask\"]\n\n    logger.info(\n        \"Extracted activations: %s from %d layers\", selected.shape, len(layer_indices)\n    )\n\n    # Free base model memory\n    del model\n    if device != \"cpu\":\n        torch.cuda.empty_cache() if \"cuda\" in device else None\n\n    return selected, attention_mask\n\n\ndef generate_adapter_from_sakana(\n    text: str,\n    output_dir: str,\n    checkpoint_path: str | Path | None = None,\n    variant: str = DEFAULT_VARIANT,\n    base_model_name: str | None = None,\n    device: str = \"cpu\",\n) -> str:\n    \"\"\"End-to-end: text → base model activations → HyperLoRA → PEFT adapter.\n\n    This is the main entry point. It:\n      1. Loads the Sakana pretrained perceiver (downloading if needed)\n      2. Runs text through the base model to get per-layer activations\n      3. Feeds activations through the perceiver to generate LoRA weights\n      4. Saves weights in PEFT-compatible format\n\n    Args:\n        text: Input text (trajectory, document, context) to encode.\n        output_dir: Directory to save the PEFT adapter files.\n        checkpoint_path: Path to local checkpoint, or None to download.\n        variant: HF checkpoint variant if downloading.\n        base_model_name: Override base model. If None, uses the one from checkpoint.\n        device: Device for computation.\n\n    Returns:\n        Path to the saved adapter directory.\n    \"\"\"\n    import torch  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n    hypernet, hc = load_sakana_checkpoint(checkpoint_path, variant, device)\n\n    # Determine base model from checkpoint config\n    if base_model_name is None:\n        # Load checkpoint just to read base_model_name_or_path\n        if checkpoint_path is None:\n            checkpoint_path = download_checkpoint(variant)\n        sd = torch.load(str(checkpoint_path), map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=False)\n        base_model_name = sd[\"base_model_name_or_path\"]\n        del sd\n\n    logger.info(\"Base model: %s\", base_model_name)\n\n    # Extract activations from base model\n    layer_indices = list(hc.layer_indices)\n    features, attn_mask = extract_activations(\n        text=text,\n        base_model_name=base_model_name,\n        layer_indices=layer_indices,\n        device=device,\n        max_length=512,\n    )\n\n    # Generate LoRA weights via perceiver\n    logger.info(\"Generating LoRA weights via HyperLoRA perceiver...\")\n    with torch.no_grad():\n        lora_dict, layernorm_dict = hypernet.generate_weights(\n            features, attn_mask, None\n        )\n\n    # Save as PEFT adapter\n    _save_sakana_adapter(\n        lora_dict=lora_dict,\n        output_dir=output_dir,\n        base_model_name=base_model_name,\n        hc=hc,\n    )\n\n    return output_dir\n\n\ndef _save_sakana_adapter(\n    lora_dict: dict[str, dict[str, Any]],\n    output_dir: str,\n    base_model_name: str,\n    hc: Any,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Save Sakana's HyperLoRA output as a PEFT-compatible adapter.\n\n    Converts from Sakana's format (dict of module → {A, B} with batch/layer dims)\n    to PEFT's flat state_dict format.\n\n    Args:\n        lora_dict: Output from HyperLoRA.generate_weights().\n        output_dir: Directory to write adapter files.\n        base_model_name: Base model identifier for adapter config.\n        hc: HypernetConfig with lora rank and target modules.\n    \"\"\"\n    from safetensors.torch import save_file  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n    output_path = Path(output_dir)\n    output_path.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n    layer_indices = list(hc.layer_indices)\n    target_modules = list(hc.lora_config.target_modules)\n    rank = hc.lora_config.r\n\n    # Convert Sakana format → PEFT flat state_dict\n    # Sakana: lora_dict[module_name][\"A\"] shape (batch, num_layers, rank, in_features)\n    # PEFT: base_model.model.model.layers.{i}.mlp.{module}.lora_A.weight (rank, in_features)\n    state_dict = {}\n    for mod_name, weights in lora_dict.items():\n        if mod_name not in target_modules:\n            continue\n        a_weights = weights[\"A\"]  # (batch, num_layers, rank, in_features)\n        b_weights = weights[\"B\"]  # (batch, num_layers, rank, out_features)\n\n        for layer_pos, layer_idx in enumerate(layer_indices):\n            key_a = (\n                f\"base_model.model.model.layers.{layer_idx}\"\n                f\".mlp.{mod_name}.lora_A.weight\"\n            )\n            key_b = (\n                f\"base_model.model.model.layers.{layer_idx}\"\n                f\".mlp.{mod_name}.lora_B.weight\"\n            )\n            # Take first batch element, transpose B to (out_features, rank)\n            state_dict[key_a] = a_weights[0, layer_pos].contiguous()\n            state_dict[key_b] = b_weights[0, layer_pos].t().contiguous()\n\n    save_file(state_dict, str(output_path / \"adapter_model.safetensors\"))\n\n    adapter_config = {\n        \"peft_type\": \"LORA\",\n        \"r\": rank,\n        \"lora_alpha\": rank * 2,\n        \"target_modules\": target_modules,\n        \"lora_dropout\": 0.0,\n        \"bias\": \"none\",\n        \"task_type\": \"CAUSAL_LM\",\n        \"base_model_name_or_path\": base_model_name,\n        \"inference_mode\": True,\n        \"modules_to_save\": None,\n        \"fan_in_fan_out\": False,\n    }\n    (output_path / \"adapter_config.json\").write_text(json.dumps(adapter_config, indent=2))\n\n    logger.info(\n        \"Saved PEFT adapter: %d tensors, %d layers, rank=%d, targets=%s\",\n        len(state_dict),\n        len(layer_indices),\n        rank,\n        target_modules,\n    )\n"
}

## Rune Pipeline Summary

I've analyzed the complete Rune pipeline. Here's the breakdown:

### 1. Inference Provider Interface
**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/inference/src/inference/provider.py`

**Key Methods:**
- `async generate(prompt: str, model: str, adapter_id: str | None = None, max_tokens: int = 1024) -> GenerationResult` — Generates text from a prompt with optional LoRA adapter
- `async load_adapter(adapter_id: str, adapter_path: str) -> None` — Loads a LoRA adapter into the inference server
- `async unload_adapter(adapter_id: str) -> None` — Unloads a previously loaded adapter
- `async list_adapters() -> list[str]` — Lists all currently loaded adapters

**Summary:** Provider-agnostic API for text generation and LoRA lifecycle; concrete implementations (VLLMProvider, OllamaProvider) differ in adapter support.

---

### 2. Agent Graph
**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/services/rune-agent/src/rune_agent/graph.py`

**Nodes:**
- `generate_node` — Generates code using the LLM
- `execute_node` — Runs code in a sandboxed environment
- `reflect_node` — Increments attempt counter and records trajectory
- `save_trajectory_node` — Saves completed trajectory for training

**Graph Variants:**
- `create_graph()` — Full retry loop: `START → generate → execute → reflect → [should_retry] → generate (retry) OR save_trajectory → END`
- `create_single_iteration_graph()` — Single iteration: `START → generate → execute → reflect → END` (used by outer rune_runner loop)
- `get_graph()` — Thread-safe singleton getter with double-checked locking

**Summary:** LangGraph workflow orchestrating code generation, execution, and reflection with conditional retry logic.

---

### 3. Agent Nodes
**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/services/rune-agent/src/rune_agent/nodes.py`

**Node Implementations:**

- **`generate_node(state)`** — Reads model/adapter from env/state, calls provider.generate() with system prompt + task description + prior errors (on retry), extracts code from markdown blocks

- **`execute_node(state)`** — Concatenates generated_code + test_suite, runs in sandbox with timeout, captures stdout/stderr/exit_code, returns tests_passed=(exit_code==0 && !timed_out)

- **`reflect_node(state)`** — Increments attempt_count, appends {generated_code, stdout, stderr, exit_code, tests_passed} to immutable trajectory list

- **`save_trajectory_node(state)`** — Calls record_trajectory() to persist trajectory, determines outcome ('success' if tests_passed else 'exhausted')

**Summary:** Each node is a discrete async step; state is immutable (returns new dicts, not mutated); reflection captures all attempt data for parametric memory training.

---

### 4. Rune Runner (Iteration Loop)
**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/scripts/rune_runner.py`

**Main Flow:**
```
run_project(project_prompt, max_iterations, checkpoint_path, ...)
  ├─ Iteration 0 (Bootstrap): load_methodology() → run_hypernetwork() → load adapter
  ├─ Iterations 1..N:
  │   ├─ run_iteration() → single-iteration graph with current adapter
  │   ├─ Accumulate trajectory from iteration output
  │   ├─ If tests_passed → break
  │   └─ run_hypernetwork() on accumulated trajectory → new adapter
  └─ Return summary (session_id, total_iterations, final_tests_passed, iterations array)
```

**Key Functions:**
- `run_iteration(graph, project_prompt, adapter_id, session_id, iteration) -> dict` — Single iteration with fixed adapter
- `run_hypernetwork(trajectory_text, output_dir, base_model_id, checkpoint_path, device) -> str | None` — Calls generate_adapter(), returns adapter path or None
- `_build_trajectory_text(state, iteration) -> str` — Formats code/stdout/stderr/test results for encoding

**Summary:** Outer loop runs 1..N iterations; each iteration produces code, hypernetwork encodes accumulated output as adapter weights, next iteration loads that adapter. The project prompt is constant; state evolves through weight-space.

---

### 5. Hypernetwork Module
**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/hypernetwork.py`

**Key Functions:**
- `DocToLoraHypernetwork(input_dim=32000, num_latents=32, latent_dim=256, depth=4, heads=8, rank=8, target_modules=["q_proj", "v_proj"], num_layers=28, hidden_dim=4096, module_dims=None)` — Perceiver-based nn.Module that generates LoRA weights in one forward pass (lazy proxy, torch deferred)

- `load_pretrained(checkpoint_path, device="cpu", **kwargs) -> nn.Module` — Loads pretrained hypernetwork from .pt checkpoint

- `trajectory_to_tokens(trajectory_text, vocab_size=32000, max_length=2048) -> Tensor` — Hash-based character trigram tokenization → token ID tensor (1, max_length)

- `generate_adapter(hypernetwork, trajectory_text, output_dir, base_model_id, vocab_size=32000, max_length=2048, device="cpu") -> str` — End-to-end: tokenize → hypernetwork forward → save_hypernetwork_adapter() → return output_dir

- `save_hypernetwork_adapter(weights, output_dir, base_model_id, rank=8, target_modules=["q_proj", "v_proj"])` — Writes adapter_model.safetensors + adapter_config.json in PEFT format

**Summary:** Perceiver-based network (cross-attention over token embeddings, self-attention stack) produces rank-8 LoRA weights in <1s per trajectory; architecture: learnable token embedding + latent array + cross-attend + self-attend + linear projection head.

---

### 6. Adapter Registry
**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/adapter-registry/src/adapter_registry/`

**CRUD Operations (AdapterRegistry class):**

**Write:**
- `store(record: AdapterRecord)` — Persist new adapter, raise AdapterAlreadyExistsError on duplicate
- `archive(adapter_id: str)` — Soft-delete by setting is_archived=True
- `update_fitness(adapter_id, pass_rate, fitness_score)` — Update evaluation metrics

**Read:**
- `retrieve_by_id(adapter_id: str) -> AdapterRecord` — Get single adapter by ID, raise AdapterNotFoundError if missing
- `query_by_task_type(task_type: str) -> list[AdapterRecord]` — Filter by task_type
- `list_all() -> list[AdapterRecord]` — All non-archived adapters
- `query_best_for_task(task_type, top_k=3) -> list[AdapterRecord]` — Top-k by fitness_score DESC
- `is_task_solved(task_hash, threshold=0.95) -> bool` — Check if any adapter meets pass_rate threshold
- `get_lineage(adapter_id) -> list[str]` — Walk parent_ids chain to root
- `query_unevaluated(task_type=None) -> list[AdapterRecord]` — Filter where pass_rate IS NULL
- `get_task_types() -> list[str]` — Distinct task types

**Model (AdapterRecord):**
- id (PK), version, task_type (indexed), base_model_id, rank, created_at, file_path, file_hash, file_size_bytes, pass_rate, fitness_score, source, session_id, is_archived, parent_ids (JSON), generation, training_task_hash (indexed), agent_id

**Summary:** SQLite-backed registry (SQLModel) for LoRA adapter lineage, evaluation, and deduplication; soft-delete + parent tracking for evolutionary trees.

---

### 7. Evaluation Module
**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/evaluation/src/evaluation/metrics.py`

**Functions:**
- `calculate_pass_at_k(n_samples: int, n_correct: int, k: int = 1) -> float` — Unbiased Pass@k estimator from HumanEval paper

- `run_kill_switch_gate(baseline_pass1, adapter_pass1, threshold=0.05) -> dict` — Compare baseline vs adapter, return verdict (PASS/FAIL) + relative_delta

- `run_humaneval_subset(adapter_id, subset_size=20, model="Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B", completions=dict) -> dict` — Run fixed 20-task HumanEval subset, return {pass_count, fail_count, pass_rate, task_results, summary}

- `score_adapter_quality(adapter_id, pass_rate, generalization_delta=None) -> float` — Aggregate pass_rate + generalization_delta into 0.0–1.0 quality score

- `compare_adapters(adapter_ids, benchmark="humaneval") -> dict` — Head-to-head comparison, return {scores, rankings, best_adapter, summary}

- `test_generalization(adapter_id, in_distribution_tasks=None, ood_tasks=None) -> dict` — Measure OOD robustness, return {in_distribution_score, ood_score, generalization_delta, generalizes}

- `evaluate_fitness(adapter_id, pass_rate, diversity_score=0.0) -> float` — Composite fitness = 0.7×pass_rate + 0.3×diversity_score

**Summary:** Benchmarking + ranking for adapters; combines performance (Pass@k), generalization, and evolutionary diversity into fitness scores.

---

## End-to-End Flow Summary

1. **Outer Loop (rune_runner):** Accumulate trajectory across iterations → hypernetwork encodes → new adapter
2. **Inner Loop (agent graph):** Single iteration generates code → executes → reflects → returns state
3. **Inference:** Provider.generate() uses current adapter_id if present, else base model
4. **Hypernetwork:** Trajectory text → tokens → LoRA weights → safetensors + config
5. **Registry:** Track lineage, fitness, task deduplication
6. **Evaluation:** HumanEval benchmark + Pass@k + fitness scoring

**Key Integration Points:**
- rune_runner calls create_single_iteration_graph() once, then iterates manually
- generate_node reads RUNE_MODEL env var, uses adapter_ids[0] from state
- execute_node runs code in RUNE_EXEC_TIMEOUT sandbox
- save_trajectory_node calls record_trajectory() (model_training module)
- run_hypernetwork() is gated by checkpoint_path; skips if missing
- Adapter lifecycle: load → inference → unload (via provider interface)

> AGENT

No — the Sakana checkpoint is architecture-specific. The `gemma_demo` checkpoint was trained to map gemma-2-2b's hidden states (2304-dim, 26 layers, `down_proj`) into LoRA weights sized for gemma. If you feed Qwen activations (different hidden dim, different layer count), it'll either crash on dimension mismatch or produce garbage.

However, Sakana's repo does have a **`qwen_4b_d2l`** checkpoint trained for `Qwen/Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507`. That would work with Qwen3-4B but not with Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B (different architecture).

The options:
1. **Use gemma-2-2b-it** with the `gemma_demo` checkpoint (best tested, 80k steps)
2. **Use Qwen3-4B-Instruct** with the `qwen_4b_d2l` checkpoint (20k steps, less trained)
3. **Use Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B** with our own randomly-initialized hypernetwork (tests the pipeline but not pretrained quality)

For a real e2e that proves the pretrained hypernetwork produces meaningful adapters, option 1 or 2 is the way to go. Which do you prefer?

## Technical Assessment: Cross-Model Transfer of Doc-to-LoRA Hypernetwork

### Architecture Analysis (from source code)

After examining the full Doc-to-LoRA source code at `/tmp/doc-to-lora/src/ctx_to_lora/`, here is a complete map of all dimension-dependent components in the hypernetwork pipeline:

**1. Context Encoder** (`ctx_encoder.py`)
- A frozen copy of the base LLM (or a separate model) that produces per-layer hidden state activations
- Output shape: `[batch, num_layers, seq_len, hidden_size]` (for `PerLayerActivations` mode)
- Dimension dependency: `hidden_size` of the encoder model

**2. Modality Projection** (`idefics2.py`, `Idefics2Perceiver.modality_projection`)
- An MLP that maps `input_size -> input_size*4 -> hidden_size` (the perceiver's internal dimension)
- This is the **key adapter layer**: it bridges `ctx_encoder_model_config.hidden_size` to the Perceiver's `hidden_size` (which equals `latent_size`, default 512)
- Shape: `Linear(input_size, input_size*4)`, `Linear(input_size*4, hidden_size)`

**3. Perceiver Encoder** (`idefics2.py`, `Idefics2PerceiverResampler`)
- Latent queries: `nn.Parameter(n_latents, hidden_size)` -- e.g. 208 x 512
- Cross-attention: Q from latents (dim=hidden_size), K/V from projected context (dim=hidden_size)
- All internal operations at `hidden_size` (512) -- **dimension-agnostic to base model**

**4. Perceiver Decoder** (another `Idefics2PerceiverResampler`)
- `n_output_queries = num_layers * num_modules * lora_r` (e.g., 26*1*8 = 208 for Gemma-2-2b)
- Latent queries: `nn.Parameter(n_output_queries, hidden_size)`
- Cross-attends to encoder output
- **Layer count dependency**: number of output queries is hardwired to `n_layers * n_modules * r`

**5. Weight Generation Head** (`hypernet.py`, `HyperLoRA.head`)
- `EinMix` layer with weight shape `[n_layers, d_latent, d_lora]`
- `d_lora = max(d_in[m] + d_out[m])` for each target module
- For `down_proj` on Gemma-2-2b: `d_in=9216` (intermediate_size), `d_out=2304` (hidden_size), so `d_lora=11520`
- **Per-layer weights**: the head has separate weight matrices per layer
- **Critical dimension dependency**: output dimension matches the base model's linear layer dimensions

**6. Bias Parameters** (`hypernet.py`)
- `bias_A[module]`: shape `[n_layers, r, d_in]` -- tied to base model's layer dimensions
- `bias_B[module]`: shape `[n_layers, r, d_out]` -- tied to base model's layer dimensions
- `scaler_A`, `scaler_B`: shape `[1, n_layers, r, 1]` -- tied to layer count

### What Blocks Direct Transfer

You **cannot** directly load a Gemma-2-2b checkpoint for Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B or 1.5B. The incompatibilities are:

| Component | Gemma-2-2b | Qwen3-4B | Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B | Qwen2.5-Coder-1.5B |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| hidden_size | 2304 | 2560 | 896 | 1536 |
| num_layers | 26 | 36 | 24 | 28 |
| intermediate_size | 9216 | 6912 (est.) | 4864 | 8960 |
| d_lora (down_proj) | 11520 | ~9472 | 5760 | 10496 |

Mismatched dimensions in:
- **Modality projection**: input linear layers (hidden_size of ctx encoder)
- **Decoder latent queries**: count = n_layers * n_modules * r
- **Head weights**: per-layer, and output dim = d_lora
- **Bias A/B**: shapes depend on d_in, d_out, n_layers

### Feasible Approaches (Ranked by Practicality)

**Approach 1: Train from scratch with frozen Perceiver core (RECOMMENDED)**

The Perceiver encoder's internal representation (512-dim latent space) is architecture-agnostic. The strategy:

1. **Replace the modality projection**: New MLP mapping Qwen-Coder hidden_size to the 512-dim Perceiver space. Initialize randomly, train.
2. **Keep the Perceiver encoder frozen**: The 8-block cross-attention encoder has learned general "document-to-latent-representation" mapping. These weights should transfer well since they operate in the abstract 512-dim space.
3. **Replace the Perceiver decoder's latent queries**: New `nn.Parameter(n_new_layers * n_modules * r, 512)`. Initialize from the pretrained decoder queries using interpolation (for layer count changes).
4. **Replace the weight generation head**: New `EinMix` with correct `[n_new_layers, d_latent, d_new_lora]` shape. Initialize randomly.
5. **Replace bias_A, bias_B, scaler_A, scaler_B**: New shapes for the target model.

**Training cost**: This requires fine-tuning, but significantly less than training from scratch since the Perceiver encoder (the bulk of the 309M parameters) stays frozen. You only train: modality projection (~4M params), decoder queries (~100K), head (~150M for 28 layers), biases (~5M). The encoder is ~150M of the 309M total, so you are training roughly half the parameters but starting from a much better initialization than random.

**Approach 2: SVD-based Cross-LoRA post-hoc transfer**

Use the existing hypernetwork as-is to generate LoRA weights for the source model (Gemma-2-2b or Qwen3-4B), then apply the [Cross-LoRA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.05232) framework to transfer those weights to the target model. Cross-LoRA uses rank-truncated SVD of base model weights to find aligned subspaces and projects LoRA weight updates between them.

Pros: No retraining of the hypernetwork at all. Data-free, ~20 minutes on consumer GPU.
Cons: Operates per-document (must run Cross-LoRA after each hypernetwork forward pass). The paper reports modest gains (0.8-5.3% relative) and "does not fully close the gap with directly trained adapters." Performance degrades more with larger architectural differences. Also, this adds latency to what is supposed to be instant adaptation.

**Approach 3: Full adapter surgery (linear projection wrappers)**

Insert learned linear projection layers around the frozen hypernetwork:
- Input: `Linear(new_hidden_size, old_hidden_size)` before modality projection
- Output: `Linear(old_d_lora, new_d_lora)` after the head, plus separate per-layer projections for A and B matrices
- Layer interpolation: for decoder queries, use nearest-neighbor or linear interpolation to map N_old output queries to N_new

This is similar to Approach 1 but keeps the entire pretrained hypernetwork frozen and just wraps it. The downside is that the output projection is very constrained -- mapping 11520-dim LoRA weights to 5760-dim weights through a linear layer is lossy and may not preserve the low-rank structure. Also, the per-layer weights in the head are specifically tuned for each layer's behavior in the source model, and a generic projection cannot account for layer-specific semantics.

**Approach 4: Train a new hypernetwork with knowledge distillation from the old one**

Use the pretrained hypernetwork as a teacher. For a shared corpus, generate (document, LoRA_weights) pairs using the source hypernetwork, then distill the relationship into a new hypernetwork for the target model using MSE loss on the generated LoRA weights (projected to the target space) plus the original context-distillation loss.

This combines the benefits of Approaches 1 and 2 but requires both the source and target base models to be available during training.

### Specific Findings on Cross-Model Transfer

1. **The paper tested on both Gemma-2-2b-it and Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507**, but each required training a separate hypernetwork from scratch. There is no published evidence of transferring a checkpoint between them.

2. **The zero-shot cross-modal (vision-to-text) transfer** demonstrated in the paper works because the VLM and text LLM share the same base architecture (Gemma-3-4b-it). The encoder hidden states project into the same representation space. This does not help for cross-architecture transfer.

3. **No Qwen-Coder specific variant exists.** No one has published doc-to-lora for coding models.

4. **Cross-LoRA (2508.05232)** is the closest published work for transferring LoRA weights across architectures. It tested Qwen2.5-1.5B, Qwen2.5-3B, Gemma-2-2B, and LLaMA-3.2-3B, showing it works but with performance gaps vs. native LoRA.

### Concrete Recommendation

For your use case (targeting Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B or 1.5B on M4 Pro 24GB):

**Use Approach 1 starting from the Qwen3-4B checkpoint** (not Gemma). Rationale:
- Qwen3 and Qwen2.5-Coder share the same architecture family (Qwen), so the Perceiver encoder's learned attention patterns over Qwen-style hidden states are more likely to transfer
- The Perceiver encoder (8 cross-attention blocks at 512-dim) is the most expensive and most general component -- keeping it frozen saves significant training time
- You need to retrain: modality projection, decoder queries, head, and biases
- Estimated trainable parameters: ~150-160M (vs 309M total)
- This should be feasible on 24GB with gradient checkpointing and bf16, since the frozen base model (Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B/1.5B) is small

**Layer interpolation strategy for decoder queries**: The decoder has `n_output_queries` latent query vectors. When going from 36 layers (Qwen3-4B) to 28 layers (Qwen2.5-Coder-1.5B), reshape the decoder queries from `[36*r, 512]` to `[36, r, 512]`, interpolate along the layer dimension to get `[28, r, 512]`, and reshape back to `[28*r, 512]`. This gives a reasonable initialization.

Sources:
- [Doc-to-LoRA paper (arxiv 2602.15902)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.15902)
- [Sakana AI Doc-to-LoRA blog post](https://pub.sakana.ai/doc-to-lora/)
- [Doc-to-LoRA source code](https://github.com/SakanaAI/doc-to-lora)
- [Cross-LoRA: Data-Free LoRA Transfer (arxiv 2508.05232)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.05232)
- [Text-to-LoRA Hypernetwork overview](https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/text-to-lora-t2l)
- [LoRA-based Hypernetworks overview](https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/lora-based-hypernetwork)

Here's the bottom line:

**You can't directly swap the checkpoint between architectures.** The dimensions are hardwired throughout — modality projection, decoder queries, weight generation head, and biases all depend on the base model's hidden size, layer count, and intermediate size.

**Best option: Approach 1 — Freeze the Perceiver core, retrain the periphery**

The Perceiver encoder (8 cross-attention blocks, 512-dim internal) is architecture-agnostic — it learned general "document → latent representation" mapping. The strategy:

1. **Keep frozen**: The Perceiver encoder (~150M params) from the `qwen_4b_d2l` checkpoint (same Qwen family)
2. **Retrain** (~150M params):
   - Modality projection (maps Qwen-Coder hidden_size → 512-dim perceiver space)
   - Decoder latent queries (interpolate from 36→24/28 layers as initialization)
   - Weight generation head (new output dims for Qwen-Coder's `down_proj`)
   - Bias parameters

This is ~50% of the parameters and benefits from the pretrained encoder's understanding of Qwen-style hidden states. Feasible on your M4 Pro 24GB with bf16.

**However** — this is a training job, not a quick config change. It needs a dataset (QA pairs run through Qwen-Coder to generate training targets).

**For the e2e test right now**, the pragmatic path is: use `google/gemma-2-2b-it` with the `gemma_demo` checkpoint (best trained, 80k steps). It's ~5GB but the VM has the bandwidth and memory. We can pursue Qwen-Coder adaptation as a separate training milestone. Want to go that route?

Now I have enough to give a comprehensive answer. Here's the full research synthesis:

---

## Sakana Doc-to-LoRA: Complete Training Architecture

### 1. Training Loop (train.py + trainer.py)

**Orchestration**: `train.py` loads the model, datasets, and delegates to `train_model()` which instantiates a HuggingFace `Trainer` subclass.

**Two trainer variants:**
- **`CrossEntropyTrainer`** -- standard causal LM CE loss with label shifting: `F.cross_entropy(logits, shift_labels, reduction="none")`
- **`DistillationTrainer`** -- KL divergence loss between student logits and teacher log-probs stored in the dataset (`logprobs_vals`, `logprobs_indices` fields)

**Loss composition:**
```
total_loss = task_loss + gen_lora_l1_reg_coef * l1_norm
```
Where L1 regularization on generated LoRA weights:
```python
l1_norm += lora["A"].abs().sum(0).mean() + lora["B"].abs().sum(0).mean()
```

**Per-context averaging**: When `use_per_ctx_average_loss=True`, losses are grouped by context (multiple QAs per document) and averaged at the context level first, then across the batch.

**Optimizer**: `adamw_torch_fused` with default HuggingFace Trainer handling. Weight decay is selectively applied -- excluded from: `nn.Embedding`, `nn.LayerNorm`, and parameters matching `"scaler"`, `"bias"`, `"layernorm"`, `"rmsnorm"`, `"latents_q"`.

### 2. Exact Hyperparameters (qwen_4b_d2l/args.yaml)

| Parameter | Value |
|---|---|
| `model_name_or_path` | `Qwen/Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507` |
| `learning_rate` | `2e-5` |
| `optim` | `adamw_torch_fused` |
| `adam_beta1/beta2` | `0.9 / 0.999` |
| `adam_epsilon` | `1e-8` |
| `weight_decay` | `0.01` |
| `max_grad_norm` | `1.0` |
| `lr_scheduler_type` | `cosine_with_min_lr` |
| `lr_scheduler_kwargs.min_lr` | `1e-7` |
| `warmup_steps` | `2000` |
| `max_steps` | `20000` |
| `per_device_train_batch_size` | `1` (packed) |
| `gradient_accumulation_steps` | `16` |
| `bf16` | `true` |
| `tf32` | `true` |
| `lora_r` | `8` |
| `lora_dropout` | `0.0` |
| `target_modules` | `["down_proj"]` |
| `gen_lora_l1_reg_coef` | `0.1` |
| `use_kl_loss` | `true` |
| `use_per_ctx_average_loss` | `true` |
| `neftune_noise_alpha` | `5.0` |
| `seed` | `42` |
| `save_steps` | `5000` |
| `logging_steps` | `100` |

**Hypernetwork architecture params:**
| Parameter | Value |
|---|---|
| `latent_size` (d_latent) | `512` |
| `light_weight_latent_size` | `128` |
| `n_latent_queries` | `8` |
| `num_latent_factor` | `8` |
| `num_blocks` | `9` |
| `num_self_attn_per_block` | `0` |
| `num_pre_head_layers` | `1` |
| `aggregator_type` | `perceiver` |
| `dropout_rate` | `0.0` |
| `pooling_type` | `mean` |
| `per_layer_processing` | `true` |
| `ctx_encoder_type` | `per_layer_activations` |
| `quantize_ctx_encoder` | `true` |
| `use_sequence_packing` | `true` |
| `use_bias` | `true` |
| `shared_weights` | `false` |

**Context/sequence lengths:**
| Parameter | Value |
|---|---|
| `max_base_len` | `8192` |
| `max_ctx_chunk_len` | `512` |
| `max_packed_ctx_len` | `2048` |
| `max_packed_inp_len` | `1024` |
| `max_qas_len` | `1024` |
| `max_qas_per_sample` | `1` |
| `min_ctx_chunk_len` | `25` |

**Context chunk count distribution** (`num_chunk_probs`): 50% single chunk, remaining split across 2-8 chunks at 6.25% each.

### 3. HyperLoRA Architecture

**Three frozen + one trainable component:**

| Component | Status |
|---|---|
| Base LLM (Qwen3-4B) | **Frozen** |
| Context encoder (same LLM, quantized) | **Frozen** |
| HyperLoRA (perceiver + MLP + heads) | **Trainable** |

**Forward pass flow:**
```
ctx_ids -> ctx_encoder (frozen, per_layer_activations)
  -> [bs, num_layers, seq_len, hidden_size]
  -> Perceiver aggregator (cross-attn with n_latent_queries=8)
  -> [bs, n_layers, n_modules, r, d_latent]     # d_latent=512, r=8
  -> ResMLPBlock layers (num_pre_head_layers=1)
  -> [bs, n_layers, n_modules, r, d_latent]
  -> L2 normalize
  -> head projection (Mix layer)
  -> [bs, n_layers, n_modules, r, d_lora]        # d_lora = max(d_in + d_out)
  -> split into A [bs, n_layers, r, d_in] and B [bs, n_layers, r, d_out]
  -> scale by scaler_A, scaler_B; add bias_A, bias_B
  -> apply_lora_to_layers() patches base LLM forward methods
  -> base LLM forward(input_ids) -> logits -> loss
```

**Named parameter groups in HyperLoRA:**

1. **Perceiver aggregator** (`hypernet.aggregator.*`):
   - Cross-attention layers, latent queries, self-attention
   - 9 blocks (`num_blocks=9`), 0 self-attention per block

2. **ResMLPBlock layers** (`hypernet.layers.*`):
   - `layers.{i}.mlp.0.weight/bias` -- LayerNorm
   - `layers.{i}.mlp.2.weight/bias` -- Linear(d_latent -> d_latent*4) = Linear(512 -> 2048)
   - `layers.{i}.mlp.5.weight/bias` -- Linear(d_latent*4 -> d_latent) = Linear(2048 -> 512)
   - `layers.{i}.mlp.6.weight/bias` -- LayerNorm

3. **LoRA biases and scalers** (`hypernet.bias_A.*`, `hypernet.bias_B.*`, `hypernet.scaler_A.*`, `hypernet.scaler_B.*`):
   - `bias_A.{module_name}`: shape `[n_layers, r, d_in[m]]` -- init `N(0, 0.2/sqrt(d_in*r))`
   - `bias_B.{module_name}`: shape `[n_layers, r, d_out[m]]` -- init zeros
   - `scaler_A.{module_name}`: shape `[1, n_layers, r, 1]` -- init ones
   - `scaler_B.{module_name}`: shape `[1, n_layers, r, 1]` -- init zeros

4. **Projection head** (`hypernet.head.*`):
   - `head.weight`: shape `[n_layers, d_latent, d_lora]` (single module) or `[n_layers, n_modules, d_latent, d_lora]`
   - Init: `N(0, 0.5/sqrt(d_latent + d_lora*r))`

**To freeze encoder and train only the rest:**
```python
# Already frozen by default in _init_model():
for p in model.ctx_encoder.parameters():
    p.requires_grad = False
for p in model.base_model.parameters():
    p.requires_grad = False

# Only hypernet params are trainable:
# model.hypernet.aggregator, model.hypernet.layers,
# model.hypernet.bias_A/B, model.hypernet.scaler_A/B, model.hypernet.head
```

The `state_dict()` override saves only trainable hypernet params and verifies base model + encoder remain frozen.

### 4. Data Format & Datasets

**Training datasets** (from args.yaml `train_ds_names`):
1. `self_gen/.../fw_qa_v2/min_0_to_2000/train/*level_1*.parquet` -- Self-generated QA from FineWeb
2. `self_gen/.../pwc_compact` -- Papers With Code
3. `self_gen/.../squad_compact` -- SQuAD
4. `self_gen/.../ropes_compact` -- ROPES
5. `self_gen/.../drop_compact` -- DROP

All are **self-generated** by Qwen3-4B-Instruct itself (`temp_0.0`). The `closed_qa_prob` controls whether QA is answerable from the context alone (1.0 = fully closed-book QA).

**Validation datasets**: `squad`, `pwc`, `drop`, `ropes`

**Raw data format** after preprocessing -- all datasets normalized to:
```python
{
    "context": str,       # The document/passage
    "prompts": list[str], # List of questions
    "responses": list[str] # List of answers
}
```

**After tokenization and packing**, each batch contains:
```python
{
    "input_ids": Tensor,       # Packed QA sequences
    "attention_mask": Tensor,
    "labels": Tensor,          # -100 for non-response tokens
    "position_ids": Tensor,    # Reset per packed example
    "ctx_ids": Tensor,         # Context token sequences (separate)
    "ctx_attn_mask": Tensor,
    "n_queries": int,          # Number of QA pairs in this pack
    "n_ctx_chunks": int,       # Number of context chunks
}
```

Context is chunked (max 512 tokens per chunk, up to 2048 total packed context) and fed separately to the context encoder. QA pairs are packed into sequences (max 1024 packed input length) with position IDs reset per example for Flash Attention.

### 5. Evaluation Metrics

Three metrics computed during eval:
- `compute_per_token_acc` -- token-level accuracy
- `compute_prefix_matching` -- prefix match rate
- `compute_perplexity` -- language modeling perplexity

The Qwen model was initialized from a prior checkpoint: `train_outputs/.../checkpoint-80000/pytorch_model.bin` (the `from_pretrained_checkpoint` field), indicating a two-stage training: first 80k steps on Gemma-2-2B, then fine-tuned for Qwen3-4B.

   291→def run_e2e() -> None:
   292→    """Run the full end-to-end test."""

   295→    print("=" * 60)

   298→    print(f"  LoRA: rank={RANK}, targets={TARGET_MODULES}")
   299→
   300→    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
   301→        checkpoint_path = os.path.join(tmpdir, "hypernetwork.pt")
   302→        adapter_dir = os.path.join(tmpdir, "test_adapter")
   303→
   304→        # Step 1: Create random hypernetwork checkpoint

   306→
   307→        # Step 2: Test hypernetwork generates adapter

   309→
   310→        # Step 3: Test provider loads and uses adapter

   312→
   313→        # Step 4: Full iteration loop
   314→        asyncio.run(test_full_iteration_loop(checkpoint_path))
   315→
   316→    print("\n" + "=" * 60)
   317→    print("  E2E TEST COMPLETE")
   318→    print("=" * 60)

   320→
